\subsection{How to run}\label{tm:suite:phase4-random-kernels:how-to-run}

\begin{lstlisting}[language=bash]
# From repo root, inside the LibreLane nix-shell:
make -C sim gpu_random                         # 1000 kernels (default)
make -C sim gpu_random_smoke                   # 25-kernel smoke (also runs inside gpu_all)
RANDOM_KERNELS=5000 make -C sim gpu_random     # custom count
RANDOM_KERNELS_SEED=0x1234 make -C sim gpu_random  # different base seed
RANDOM_KERNELS_SEED=0xBAD0 RANDOM_KERNELS=1 make -C sim gpu_random  # reproduce a failing seed

# Full rollup (unit + kernels + handoff + random smoke):
make -C sim gpu_all

# Nightly regression (CPU + cache + GPU + CPU/GPU random):
make -C sim regression_full
\end{lstlisting}

\code{gpu_random} sets \texttt{EXTRA\_\allowbreak{}ARGS=\allowbreak{}""}
to disable Verilator \code{--trace}, so no multi-GB \code{dump.vcd}.

\subsection{Methodology}\label{tm:suite:phase4-random-kernels:methodology}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{DUT:} \code{gpu_top} (full GPU: command queue, scheduler,
  compute unit, regfile, ALU, memory unit + coalescer, shared memory).
\item
  \textbf{Reference model:} \src{tb/cocotb/gpu/gpu_ref_model.py}
  (\code{GpuRefModel}), a Python interpreter that matches the actual
  RTL/\code{gpu_asm} opcode encoding (\code{gpu_pkg.sv}) and
  \code{vector_alu.sv} semantics exactly (VMUL lower-32, VSRL logical,
  VSRA arithmetic, sign-extended 12-bit immediates, r0 hardwired). As of
  2026-06-01 the model also implements:

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Single-level SIMT divergence stack (\code{VBEQ/VBNE/VBLT/VBGE},
    \code{VJMP}): per-lane active mask, taken/\allowbreak{}not-taken
    split, reconvergence via stack pop on VRET. Semantics validated
    against \code{test_divergence.py} and the \code{gpu_compute_unit.sv}
    divergence stack contract.
  \item
    \code{initial_gmem} parameter: pre-seeds global memory before
    execution so VLD reads return deterministic values. The model
    returns \code{gmem_writes} (VST destinations only), not the
    pre-seeded read-only input entries.
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Generator} (\code{test_random_kernels.py}, seeded per kernel
  for reproducibility): each kernel is a single 8-lane warp with these
  building blocks applied probabilistically:
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{Kernel building blocks}\label{tm:suite:phase4-random-kernels:kernel-building-blocks}

{\small\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}

{
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*312/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*116/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*552/1000\relax}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Block
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Probability
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
What it exercises
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
Straight-line ALU + global stores & always & ALU R/I ops, regfile,
global store (memory unit + coalescer + AXI), VSYNC (NOP) \\
\textbf{B1 global loads} & 40\% & VLD from \code{INPUT_REGION} (0x100),
address calculation, feed loaded values into compute \\
\textbf{B2 shared memory} & 40\% & VSTS → VSYNC barrier → VLDS
round-trip; smem bank addressing; feed back into compute + VST \\
\textbf{B3 divergence diamond} & 35\% & VBLT with per-lane signed
compare, single-level SIMT push/pop, masked VST on each path \\
\end{longtable}
}

}

A single kernel may receive 0, 1, 2, or all 3 extensions simultaneously.

\subsubsection{Address layout}\label{tm:suite:phase4-random-kernels:address-layout}

\begin{textblock}
Global address space:
0x000–0x0FF : (unused — VRET fallback returns 0x3F from instr_responder)
0x100–0x11F : INPUT_REGION — 8 words (one per lane); pre-seeded; read-only VLD source
0x200–0x23F : STORE_REGIONS[0] — baseline store region (also B3 unreachable-tail store)
0x240–0x27F : STORE_REGIONS[1] — baseline store, or B3 false-path diamond store
0x280–0x2BF : STORE_REGIONS[2] — baseline store, or B3 true-path diamond store

Shared address space (separate from global):
0x000–0x1FF : SMEM (shared scratchpad) — 512 B window used (physical 16 KB / 32
              banks); lane writes smem[lane*4]
\end{textblock}

Regions are non-overlapping. Store lane stride is 4 bytes; 8 lanes = 32
bytes per region, well within the 64-byte region spacing.

\subsubsection{Scoreboard}\label{tm:suite:phase4-random-kernels:scoreboard}

After each kernel:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \code{gpu_wait_done} (timeout=5000 cycles) detects deadlocks.
\item
  \code{data_mem} (AXI responder dict) is compared against
  \texttt{expected\ =\allowbreak{}\ GpuRefModel(.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}).\allowbreak{}run(words)}:

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    \code{expected} = \code{gmem_writes} only (VST destinations; no
    pre-seeded read entries).
  \item
    \code{rtl_stores} = \code{data_mem} minus \code{input_gmem}
    addresses (pre-seeded reads are not RTL writes).
  \item
    Both sets must have equal cardinality and matching values at every
    address.
  \end{itemize}
\end{enumerate}

\subsubsection{Divergence diamond shape}\label{tm:suite:phase4-random-kernels:divergence-diamond-shape}

The B3 generator always emits a provably-reconverging,
provably-terminating, single-level diamond:

\begin{textblock}
VBLT rs1=working_reg, rs2=r0, target=true_path   ; push(false_path_pc, not_taken_mask)
; fall-through (false-path, lanes where working_reg[l] >= 0):
  VADDI  div_reg, r0, false_val
  VADDI  R_BASE, r0, (STORE_REGIONS[1] - div_reg_idx)
  VADD   R_BASE, R_BASE, R_OFF
  VST    div_reg, R_BASE, 0                        ; mem[STORE_REGIONS[1] + l*4] = false_val
  VRET                                              ; end false-path
; [pad VRETs to align true_path_pc to branch_pc+32]
true_path:
  VADDI  div_reg, r0, true_val
  VADDI  R_BASE, r0, (STORE_REGIONS[2] - div_reg_idx)
  VADD   R_BASE, R_BASE, R_OFF
  VST    div_reg, R_BASE, 0                        ; mem[STORE_REGIONS[2] + l*4] = true_val
  VRET                                              ; pop → execute false-path lanes → VRET
\end{textblock}

Branch offset = 32 bytes (multiple of 32, so \code{rs2=r0} index=0
satisfies the branch encoding constraint
\texttt{offset[4:\allowbreak{}0]\ =\allowbreak{}=\allowbreak{}\ rs2\_\allowbreak{}index}).
Branch condition
\texttt{VBLT\ rs1,\allowbreak{}\ rs2=\allowbreak{}r0,\allowbreak{}\ .\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}}
compares signed \texttt{rs1[l]\ <\ 0}, which splits the 8 lanes based on
the sign of whatever accumulated value is in \code{rs1}. If all lanes
happen to agree (all same sign after the compute phase), the branch
converges --- the model and RTL both handle converging branches
correctly.

\subsection{Scope rationale}\label{tm:suite:phase4-random-kernels:scope-rationale}

{\small\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}

{
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-2\tabcolsep)*474/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-2\tabcolsep)*506/1000\relax}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Feature
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Covered by
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
ALU R-type
(VADD/\allowbreak{}VSUB/\allowbreak{}VMUL/\allowbreak{}VAND/\allowbreak{}VOR/\allowbreak{}VXOR/\allowbreak{}VSLL/\allowbreak{}VSRL/\allowbreak{}VSRA)
& random kernel (always) \\
ALU I-type
(VADDI/\allowbreak{}VANDI/\allowbreak{}VORI/\allowbreak{}VXORI) & random
kernel (always) \\
VMOV\_\allowbreak{}TID\_\allowbreak{}X & random kernel (always, init) \\
Global store (VST) via AXI4 + coalescer & random kernel (always) \\
VSYNC (single-warp NOP) & random kernel (always, low probability) \\
Global load (VLD) & B1 (40\% probability per kernel) \\
Shared-memory round-trip (VSTS/\allowbreak{}VLDS) & B2 (40\% probability
per kernel) \\
Single-level divergence (VBLT, SIMT stack) & B3 (35\% probability per
kernel) \\
Multi-level divergence, loops, backward branches & directed tests only
(\code{kernel_divergence_basic}, \code{test_divergence.py}) ---
intentionally excluded from random generator \\
Multi-warp scheduling & directed tests (\code{test_warp_scheduler.py},
\code{kernel_sync}) \\
\end{longtable}
}

}

Restricting the random space to the exactly-modeled subset keeps every
mismatch a genuine RTL datapath bug rather than a
generator/\allowbreak{}model artifact, while the 1000-seed campaign
exercises broad
ALU/\allowbreak{}regfile/\allowbreak{}load/\allowbreak{}store/\allowbreak{}smem/\allowbreak{}divergence
combinations.

\subsection{Reproducing a specific seed}\label{tm:suite:phase4-random-kernels:reproducing-a-specific-seed}

\begin{lstlisting}[language=bash]
RANDOM_KERNELS_SEED=0xBAD0 RANDOM_KERNELS=1 make -C sim gpu_random
\end{lstlisting}

The first 10 failing seeds are also logged as \code{ERROR} lines in the
cocotb output, so reproduction does not require external tracking.
\end{relocated}

\begin{relocated}{docs/development/CONTROL_SIGNALS_ANALYSIS.md}
% Converted from docs/development/CONTROL_SIGNALS_ANALYSIS.md by tools/docs/md2tex.py; edit this file, not the original.
\section{\texorpdfstring{Control Signal Analysis for
rv32i\_\allowbreak{}decode.sv}{Control Signal Analysis for rv32i\_decode.sv}}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis}

\subsection{Overview}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:overview}

This document analyzes additional control signals that may be needed for
the RV32I decoder beyond what\textquotesingle s in the EXAMPLE decoder.

\subsection{Currently Proposed Signals (in EXAMPLE)}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:currently-proposed-signals-in-example}

{
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}ccc@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Signal & Width & Purpose \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\code{rs1}, \code{rs2}, \code{rd} & 5 bits each & Register addresses \\
\code{alu_op} & 4 bits & ALU operation selection \\
\code{alu_src_a} & 1 bit & ALU input A mux (rs1 vs PC) \\
\code{alu_src_b} & 1 bit & ALU input B mux (rs2 vs imm) \\
\code{imm_fmt} & 3 bits & Immediate format for generator \\
\code{reg_wr_en} & 1 bit & Register write enable \\
\code{mem_rd}, \code{mem_wr} & 1 bit each & Memory
read/\allowbreak{}write enables \\
\code{mem_size} & 3 bits & Memory access size \\
\code{mem_unsigned} & 1 bit & Unsigned load flag \\
\code{branch} & 1 bit & Branch instruction flag \\
\code{branch_op} & 3 bits & Branch operation type \\
\code{jump} & 1 bit & Jump instruction flag \\
\code{jalr} & 1 bit & JALR vs JAL \\
\code{illegal} & 1 bit & Illegal instruction flag \\
\end{longtable}
}

\subsection{Potentially Missing Signals}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:potentially-missing-signals}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\code{mem_to_reg} (Write-Back Mux
Control)}{(Write-Back Mux Control)}}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:1-mem-to-reg-write-back-mux-control}\origsec{1}

\textbf{Purpose}: Selects the source for register writeback value

\textbf{Encoding} (proposed):

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{2'b00} = ALU result
\item
  \texttt{2'b01} = Memory read data (for loads)
\item
  \texttt{2'b10} = PC + 4 (for JAL/JALR)
\item
  \texttt{2'b11} = Immediate (for LUI - may not be needed if ALU can
  pass through)
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Importance}: \textbf{*}\textbf{*}\textbf{*} \textbf{CRITICAL}

\textbf{Why it\textquotesingle s critical}:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Load instructions need to write memory data to register, not ALU
  result
\item
  JAL/JALR need to write PC+4 to link register
\item
  Without this, all register writes would get ALU result
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Example usage}:

\begin{lstlisting}[language=Verilog]
// In writeback stage
always_comb begin
  case (mem_to_reg)
    2'b00: reg_wr_data = alu_result;
    2'b01: reg_wr_data = mem_read_data;
    2'b10: reg_wr_data = pc_plus_4;
    2'b11: reg_wr_data = immediate;
  endcase
end
\end{lstlisting}

\textbf{Instructions that need it}:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{mem\_\allowbreak{}to\_\allowbreak{}reg\ =\allowbreak{}\ 2'b01}:
  LB, LH, LW, LBU, LHU (all loads)
\item
  \texttt{mem\_\allowbreak{}to\_\allowbreak{}reg\ =\allowbreak{}\ 2'b10}:
  JAL, JALR
\item
  \texttt{mem\_\allowbreak{}to\_\allowbreak{}reg\ =\allowbreak{}\ 2'b00}:
  All ALU operations (default)
\item
  \texttt{mem\_\allowbreak{}to\_\allowbreak{}reg\ =\allowbreak{}\ 2'b11}:
  LUI (if not using ALU passthrough)
\end{itemize}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\code{pc_wr_en} (Program Counter Write
Enable)}{(Program Counter Write Enable)}}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:2-pc-wr-en-program-counter-write-enable}\origsec{2}

\textbf{Purpose}: Controls when the PC register is updated

\textbf{Encoding}:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{1'b0} = Hold PC (stall)
\item
  \texttt{1'b1} = Update PC
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Importance}: \textbf{*}\textbf{*} \textbf{IMPORTANT} (but may be
handled by control FSM instead)

\textbf{Why it matters}:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Needed to stall PC during AXI memory transactions
\item
  Prevents PC from incrementing during multi-cycle operations
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Alternative approach}: The control FSM
(rv32i\_\allowbreak{}control.sv) could directly manage PC updates based
on FSM state, making this decoder signal unnecessary.

\textbf{Recommendation}: \bad{} \textbf{NOT needed in decoder} - This is
better handled by the control FSM which knows about AXI stalls and
pipeline state.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\code{pc_src} (Program Counter Source
Mux)}{(Program Counter Source Mux)}}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:3-pc-src-program-counter-source-mux}\origsec{3}

\textbf{Purpose}: Selects the next PC value

\textbf{Encoding} (proposed):

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{2'b00} = PC + 4 (sequential)
\item
  \texttt{2'b01} = PC + immediate (branches, JAL)
\item
  \texttt{2'b10} = rs1 + immediate (JALR)
\item
  \texttt{2'b11} = TRAP\_\allowbreak{}VECTOR (traps)
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Importance}: \textbf{*}\textbf{*}\textbf{*} \textbf{CRITICAL}

\textbf{Why it\textquotesingle s critical}:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Determines control flow for branches and jumps
\item
  Required for implementing branch/\allowbreak{}jump instructions
  correctly
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Note}: The EXAMPLE decoder has \code{pc_src} as 1-bit, but needs
to be expanded to 2 bits.

\textbf{Instructions that need it}:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{pc\_\allowbreak{}src\ =\allowbreak{}\ 2'b00}: All
  non-control-flow instructions
\item
  \texttt{pc\_\allowbreak{}src\ =\allowbreak{}\ 2'b01}: JAL, taken
  branches (BEQ, BNE, BLT, BGE, BLTU, BGEU)
\item
  \texttt{pc\_\allowbreak{}src\ =\allowbreak{}\ 2'b10}: JALR
\item
  \texttt{pc\_\allowbreak{}src\ =\allowbreak{}\ 2'b11}: Illegal
  instruction trap (set by control FSM, not decoder)
\end{itemize}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\code{result_src} or \code{wb_src}
(Alternative name for
mem\_\allowbreak{}to\_\allowbreak{}reg)}{or  (Alternative name for mem\_to\_reg)}}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:4-result-src-or-wb-src-alternative-name-for-mem-to-reg}\origsec{4}

\textbf{Same as \code{mem_to_reg}} - just different naming conventions.

Some designs use:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \code{mem_to_reg} (emphasizes memory-to-register path)
\item
  \code{result_src} (more generic, emphasizes result selection)
\item
  \code{wb_src} (writeback source)
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Recommendation}: Use \code{mem_to_reg} as it\textquotesingle s
more descriptive.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\code{alu_zero} (ALU Zero
Output)}{(ALU Zero Output)}}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:5-alu-zero-alu-zero-output}\origsec{5}

\textbf{Purpose}: Flag indicating ALU result is zero

\textbf{Importance}: \bad{} \textbf{NOT needed} as separate decoder
signal

\textbf{Why}:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  This is generated by the ALU itself, not the decoder
\item
  Used internally for some branch implementations
\item
  Since we have a separate branch comparator, not needed
\end{itemize}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\code{trap} or \code{exception}
(Trap/\allowbreak{}Exception Flag)}{or  (Trap/Exception Flag)}}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:6-trap-or-exception-trapexception-flag}\origsec{6}

\textbf{Purpose}: Indicates instruction causes a trap

\textbf{Importance}: \textbf{*}\textbf{*} \textbf{IMPORTANT}

\textbf{Overlap with existing signals}: The EXAMPLE decoder already has
\code{illegal} flag. This covers the main trap case in Phase 1.

\textbf{Additional trap sources} (may need signals):

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Misaligned memory access (could be checked by control logic)
\item
  AXI error response (detected during memory stage, not decode)
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Recommendation}: \ok{} Current \code{illegal} signal is
sufficient for Phase 1. Control FSM handles other trap conditions.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsection{Summary and Recommendations}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:summary-and-recommendations}

\subsubsection{Must Add (Critical)}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:must-add-critical}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \ok{} \textbf{\code{mem_to_reg[1:0]}} - MUST add to decoder

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Without this, loads and jumps cannot write correct data to registers
  \item
    Affects: LB, LH, LW, LBU, LHU, JAL, JALR (12 instructions)
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \ok{} \textbf{\code{pc_src[1:0]}} - MUST expand from 1-bit to 2-bits

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Need to distinguish PC+4, PC+imm, rs1+imm
  \item
    Affects: JAL, JALR, all 6 branches (8 instructions)
  \end{itemize}
\end{enumerate}

\subsubsection{Should NOT Add (Handled Elsewhere)}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:should-not-add-handled-elsewhere}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \bad{} \textbf{\code{pc_wr_en}} - Control FSM handles this based on
  state
\item
  \bad{} \textbf{\code{alu_zero}} - Generated by ALU or branch
  comparator
\item
  \bad{} \textbf{Additional trap flags} - \code{illegal} covers Phase 1
  needs
\end{enumerate}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsection{Updated Decoder Interface Proposal}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:updated-decoder-interface-proposal}

\begin{textblock}
module rv32i_decode (
  // Instruction input
  input  logic [31:0] instruction,

  // Decoded register addresses
  output logic [4:0]  rs1,
  output logic [4:0]  rs2,
  output logic [4:0]  rd,

  // ALU control
  output logic [3:0]  alu_op,
  output logic        alu_src_a,    // 0=rs1, 1=PC
  output logic        alu_src_b,    // 0=rs2, 1=immediate

  // Immediate format
  output logic [2:0]  imm_fmt,

  // Register file control
  output logic        reg_wr_en,

  // Memory control
  output logic        mem_rd,
  output logic        mem_wr,
  output logic [2:0]  mem_size,
  output logic        mem_unsigned,

  // Branch/Jump control
  output logic        branch,
  output logic [2:0]  branch_op,
  output logic        jump,
  output logic        jalr,

  // Writeback and PC control
  output logic [1:0]  mem_to_reg,   // ⭐ NEW: 00=ALU, 01=MEM, 10=PC+4, 11=IMM
  output logic [1:0]  pc_src,       // ⭐ EXPANDED: 00=PC+4, 01=PC+imm, 10=rs1+imm, 11=trap

  // Exception
  output logic        illegal
);
\end{textblock}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsection{Instruction-by-Instruction Control Signal Table}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:instruction-by-instruction-control-signal-table}

{\small\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}

{
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\centering\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)*450/1000\relax}
  >{\centering\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)*160/1000\relax}
  >{\centering\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)*174/1000\relax}
  >{\centering\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6\tabcolsep)*196/1000\relax}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\centering
Instruction
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\centering
mem\_\allowbreak{}to\_\allowbreak{}reg
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\centering
pc\_src
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\centering
Notes
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\textbf{ALU ops} (ADD, SUB, AND, OR, XOR, SLL, SRL, SRA, SLT, SLTU) & 00
(ALU) & 00 (PC+4) & Standard ALU operation \\
\textbf{ALU-imm} (ADDI, ANDI, ORI, XORI, SLLI, SRLI, SRAI, SLTI, SLTIU)
& 00 (ALU) & 00 (PC+4) & Standard ALU operation \\
\textbf{Loads} (LB, LH, LW, LBU, LHU) & 01 (MEM) & 00 (PC+4) & Write
memory data to rd \\
\textbf{Stores} (SB, SH, SW) & XX (no wr) & 00 (PC+4) & No register
write \\
\textbf{Branches} (BEQ, BNE, BLT, BGE, BLTU, BGEU) & XX (no wr) & 01 if
taken, 00 if not & Conditional PC update \\
\textbf{JAL} & 10 (PC+4) & 01 (PC+imm) & Save return address \\
\textbf{JALR} & 10 (PC+4) & 10 (rs1+imm) & Save return address \\
\textbf{LUI} & 11 (IMM) or 00 (ALU) & 00 (PC+4) & Depends on
implementation \\
\textbf{AUIPC} & 00 (ALU) & 00 (PC+4) & ALU computes PC+imm \\
\end{longtable}
}

}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsection{Questions for Human Review}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:questions-for-human-review}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \textbf{LUI implementation}: Should LUI use
  \texttt{mem\_\allowbreak{}to\_\allowbreak{}reg\ =\allowbreak{}\ 11}
  (pass immediate directly), or
  \texttt{mem\_\allowbreak{}to\_\allowbreak{}reg\ =\allowbreak{}\ 00}
  (use ALU with special handling)?

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Option A: ALU has passthrough mode when
    alu\_\allowbreak{}src\_\allowbreak{}a=0 and alu\_\allowbreak{}op=ADD
    with rs1=x0
  \item
    Option B: Separate immediate path to writeback mux
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{AUIPC implementation}: Currently uses ALU with
  alu\_\allowbreak{}src\_\allowbreak{}a=PC. Is this acceptable?
\item
  \textbf{Trap handling}: Should
  \texttt{pc\_\allowbreak{}src\ =\allowbreak{}\ 11} be set by decoder
  when \texttt{illegal\ =\allowbreak{}\ 1}, or should control FSM
  override pc\_src when handling traps?
\item
  \textbf{Branch not-taken}: When branch is not taken, should decoder
  output \texttt{pc\_\allowbreak{}src\ =\allowbreak{}\ 00}, or should
  this be decided by control logic combining \code{branch} and
  \code{branch_taken} signals?
\end{enumerate}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsection{Recommendation}\label{tm:flow:control-signals-analysis:recommendation}

\textbf{Add these two signals to rv32i\_\allowbreak{}decode.sv}:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \code{mem_to_reg[1:0]} - Critical for loads and jumps
\item
  Expand \code{pc_src} to \code{pc_src[1:0]} - Critical for JALR
\end{enumerate}

Without these, the CPU cannot correctly execute 12 instructions (5 loads
+ JAL + JALR + potentially LUI).
\end{relocated}

\section{\texttt{sim/Makefile}}\label{tm:suite:sim-makefile}

This section holds comment text that was in the source file. The text is unchanged. Each line has no leading comment marker and no indentation.

\begin{relocated}{sim/Makefile}

\subsection*{Before: export PATH := \$(HOME)/.local/bin:\$(PATH)}

\begin{textblock}
Makefile for RV32I CPU cocotb simulation
Uses Verilator by default

NOTE: For simulation tests, run this Makefile in WSL:
  From Windows: wsl bash -c "cd \$(wslpath '\$(pwd)') && make test"
  From WSL: make test

For lint check, can run from Windows or WSL:
  make lint
\end{textblock}

\subsection*{Before: SRAM\_TARGET ?= freepdk45}

\begin{textblock}
RTL source files (Phase 3: 5-stage pipeline + I-cache + D-cache)
Packages must appear first so Verilator can resolve them.

SRAM model selection — mirrors the `ifdef SRAM_SKY130 switch in cache RTL:
  SRAM_TARGET=freepdk45 (default): OpenRAM FreePDK45 model, no define needed
  SRAM_TARGET=sky130             : Sky130 behavioral model, adds +define+SRAM_SKY130

Examples:
  make lint                          # FreePDK45 (default)
  make lint SRAM_TARGET=sky130       # Sky130
  make test SRAM_TARGET=sky130       # run full suite against sky130 SRAM
\end{textblock}

\subsection*{Before: ifdef COVERAGE}

\begin{textblock}
Opt-in line + toggle coverage (GH #216 / bead nkj7 for the SoC flow, bead 1eyv here).  Same
flags as tb/cocotb/soc/Makefile so the .dat files from the two flows are point-for-point
comparable and tools/verif/coverage_report.py can merge them:
 * --coverage-line --coverage-toggle --coverage-max-width 256 instead of the blanket
   --coverage (which adds user-coverage pages the report ignores anyway);
 * the flags go in COMPILE_ARGS: several targets pass EXTRA_ARGS= on the command line, which
   would override a makefile-level `+=`;
 * `_cov` is appended to EVERY SIM_BUILD (the per-target ones passed on the command line and the
   default), so an instrumented build can never reuse an uninstrumented Vtop or vice versa
   (the stale-Vtop false-pass class of PR #142).  `override` is what lets the makefile edit a
   command-line value;
 * each simulation writes ITS OWN .dat to $(COV_DIR) through the runtime plusarg
   +verilator+coverage+file+ (cocotb 1.9.2 honours it).  The old behaviour -- every simulation
   writing sim/coverage.dat -- silently lost all but the last Phase 3/GPU run.
\end{textblock}

\subsection*{Before: VERIBLE\_LINT    := verible-verilog-lint}

\begin{textblock}
============================================================================
Verible RTL style lint (Phase 5 — complements Verilator lint)
----------------------------------------------------------------------------
Verible covers SystemVerilog *style consistency*, which Verilator's semantic
--lint-only does not. `nix develop` provides the same binary CI pins.
  make lint-verible          # style-lint every rtl/**/*.sv (curated ruleset)
  make verible               # alias for lint-verible

There are deliberately NO format targets (removed 2026-10-10, bead hn9l
follow-up). verible-verilog-format cannot reproduce the hand-aligned house
style: on v0.0-4063, 81 of 83 rtl/**/*.sv fail `--verify`, no flag set gets
below 72 of 83, and the exemplars timer.sv / gpio_controller.sv fail every
variant. An in-place `--inplace` target would be a style-only mass edit of
verified RTL, which CLAUDE.md forbids.
============================================================================
\end{textblock}

\subsection*{Before: CDC\_DIR     := \$(PROJ\_ROOT)/sim/build/cdc}

\begin{textblock}
============================================================================
CDC check — BerkeleyLab/Bedrock cdc_snitch (informational; see tools/cdc/)
============================================================================
sv2v -> yosys (flatten) -> cdc_snitch.py -> tools/cdc/cdc_gate.py.

cdc_snitch's raw BAD count is not the verdict: it models every top-level port
as its own clock domain and cannot tell a clock-gate output from its source
clock, so on this SoC ~98% of raw BADs are modelling artifacts. cdc_gate.py
canonicalizes domains (tools/cdc/cdc_config.yml, mirroring
pnr/constraints/phase5_soc_multiclock.sdc) and applies explicit justified
waivers; its exit status is the gate. `make cdc CDC_STRICT=1` propagates it.
Full writeup: Doc: tm:flow:cdc-snitch-poc
\end{textblock}

\subsection*{Before: .PHONY: sv2v-parse}

\begin{textblock}
sv2v-parse: Verilog-2005 portability gate (bead f7vs.12).
Converts the SoC sources with sv2v and parses + elaborates the result with
yosys. No CDC analysis, no synthesis -- seconds once the tools are cached.

Exists because Verilator accepts constructs sv2v mistranslates, so the entire
cocotb regression can be green while the design is unsynthesisable. A real
instance shipped in PWM and broke CI (PR #198, fixed in 2c5f351); it would
equally have broken both PD flows, which are sv2v-fronted.

The flist is regenerated from $(CDC_SOURCES) on every run, deliberately: a
stale list silently omits new modules and the check then passes without
examining them.
\end{textblock}

\subsection*{Before: .PHONY: coverage\_cpu\_gpu}

\begin{textblock}
Bead 1eyv: CPU + Phase 3 cache + Phase 4 GPU suites, instrumented, merged into ONE file
($(COV_DIR)/cpu_gpu_merged.dat) for tools/verif/coverage_report.py to combine with the SoC
regression's merged.dat.  Same Verilator, same flags, same report script as `soc_coverage`, so
the two inputs agree on point identity.  Separate from `coverage` so the nightly rtl-coverage
job's runtime and artifact are unchanged.  Fails (no `|| true`) if a suite fails or nothing was
written.
  make SIM_BUILD_ROOT=/nobackup/claude_sim_build/cov_cpu_gpu coverage_cpu_gpu
\end{textblock}

\subsection*{Before: HLS\_HAZARD\_UNIT\_V = \$(HLS\_OUT)/rv32i\_hazard\_unit/rv32i\_h...}

\begin{textblock}
rv32i_hazard_unit, HLS arm (GH #119 bead `gvr`): the SAME cocotb test
module as cpu_hazard_unit above, run against Bambu-generated Verilog
wrapped in a port-adapting + clock/reset/start/done handshake shim. The
testbench branches on the protocol difference in exactly one place
(HazardUnitDriver in test_hazard_unit.py); every vector and expected value
is identical for both arms.

TOPLEVEL is the SHIM (rv32i_hazard_unit_hls), not the reference name, same
convention as gpu_coalescer_hls below.

The generated .v and the shim are produced in parallel (not yet committed
when this target was authored) -- run the HLS flow first if this guard fires.
Recursive (=), not immediate (:=): HLS_OUT is defined later in this file
(gpu_coalescer_hls section) and must still be picked up here.
\end{textblock}

\subsection*{Before: GLS\_NETLIST ?=}

\begin{textblock}
vector_alu, gate-level sim of a POST-SYNTHESIS ASAP7 netlist (GH #119 beads
egt/gcd/b0t -- settling functional correctness between USE_SYNLIG:true and
USE_SYNLIG:false+sv2v synthesis frontends). Same unmodified cocotb suite as
gpu_alu_hls above; the netlist top module (vector_alu_hls) already exposes
the same ports as the pre-synthesis shim, so no separate shim/pkg is needed
-- GLS_NETLIST must be a self-contained Verilog file with real gate
functionality (e.g. yosys `read_liberty -ignore_miss_func <libs>;
read_verilog <nl.v>; hierarchy -top vector_alu_hls; proc; opt_clean;
write_verilog`, NOT `flatten`+`opt -full` -- flattening ASAP7 cells to raw
yosys primitives explodes a ~50-90K-instance netlist into hundreds of
thousands of gates and both Verilator's front end and the g++ build have
been observed to not complete; substituting each liberty cell as its own
small hierarchical module keeps instance COUNT the same but module TYPE
count near ~100, which is what Verilator actually struggles with).
GLS_BUILD_TAG picks the SIM_BUILD subdirectory so different netlists don't
clobber each other's Verilator build.
\end{textblock}

\subsection*{Before: HLS\_OUT ?= /nobackup/hls/out}

\begin{textblock}
memory_coalescer, HLS arm (GH #119 bead cfi): the SAME cocotb tests as
gpu_coalescer above, run against Bambu-generated Verilog wrapped in a
port-adapting shim. Running both arms through one unchanged testbench is what
makes the equivalence claim mean anything.

Two deliberate differences from the hand-RTL target:
  * a distinct SIM_BUILD, so the two arms do not invalidate each other's
    ~100 MB Verilator build dir on every alternation;
  * EXTRA_VERILATOR_FLAGS, because machine-generated Verilog does not lint
    clean (20 warnings at Verilator's default, 0 errors) and this repo does not
    hold code it did not author to its own bar. The shim IS held to it.

TOPLEVEL is the SHIM (memory_coalescer_hls), not the reference name: Bambu's
generated core is itself called `memory_coalescer`, and renaming machine-output
on every build is one more thing to break silently. The testbench never refers
to a module name -- only to dut.<signal> -- so the two coexist happily.

The generated .v is not committed; run `make -C hls memory_coalescer` (inside
`nix develop .#hls`) to produce it first.
\end{textblock}

\end{relocated}

\section{\texttt{tb/models/aes128\_model.py}}\label{tm:suite:aes128-model}

This section holds comment text that was in the source file. The text is unchanged. Each line has no leading comment marker and no indentation.

\begin{relocated}{tb/models/aes128_model.py}

\subsection*{Module docstring}

\begin{textblock}
aes128_model.py -- pure-Python golden model of the CRYPTO peripheral's AES-128 datapath
(rtl/periph/aes128_core.sv + the CTR wrapper logic in rtl/periph/crypto_accel.sv; bead
claude_verilog_test-f7vs.10, Phase 6b, Doc: ds:ch:phase6-ip-expansion-plan Sec.6b "CRYPTO").
\end{textblock}

\subsection*{Before: from \_\_future\_\_ import annotations}

\begin{textblock}
STANDARD LIBRARY ONLY. requirements.txt is deliberately untouched: the SHA-256 half of the
peripheral is checked against `hashlib.sha256`, and this module is the AES half's counterpart. It
is a clear, table-free-where-possible FIPS-197 implementation, NOT a fast or constant-time one --
it is a test oracle, never production crypto, and it has no side-channel resistance whatsoever.

This module is the authoritative specification of what the RTL must compute; the RTL implements
what this file computes. It does NOT model the peripheral's cycle timing, its register bank or its
handshake -- only the arithmetic (tb/cocotb/soc/test_crypto.py pins the rest).

BYTE ORDER (the contract the whole peripheral and its suite hang on)
--------------------------------------------------------------------
FIPS-197 / FIPS-180-4 big-endian, consistently. A 128-bit block is the 16-byte string B[0..15] as
it appears in the standard's hex notation; the 32-bit register words carry it with word 0 holding
B0 in bits [31:24]:

word 0 = B0<<24 | B1<<16 | B2<<8 | B3      (DIN0 / KEY0 / IV0 / DOUT0)
word 3 = B12<<24 | B13<<16 | B14<<8 | B15  (DIN3 / KEY3 / IV3 / DOUT3)

so the FIPS-197 hex strings, `bytes.fromhex`, `hashlib.sha256().digest()` and the register words
line up with NO byte swap anywhere. `block_to_words` / `words_to_block` are the only place the
conversion lives. The AES state is column-major: state byte index 4*c + r is row r of column c,
so bytes 0..3 are column 0 and ShiftRows is a fixed re-wiring of the 16-byte vector.

CTR MODE (NIST SP 800-38A Sec.6.5, Appendix B.1 "INC32")
--------------------------------------------------------
The peripheral exposes ECB and CTR over one ENCRYPT-ONLY datapath; CTR is what makes it a complete
cipher in both directions.

keystream[i] = AES-128-Encrypt(K, counter[i])         counter[0] = the IV register block
output[i]    = input[i] XOR keystream[i]              (the XOR happens at the writeback)
counter[i+1] = INC32(counter[i])

INC32 increments ONLY the rightmost 32-bit word (IV3), modulo 2**32. The carry out of that word is
DISCARDED: IV0..IV2 never change, so a counter that crosses 2**32 blocks wraps to the same
counter-block prefix. Software must not run one counter past a 2**32-block wrap. CTR decryption
is bit-identical to CTR encryption (S ^ (S ^ P) = P) -- there is no decrypt mode and no inverse
S-box anywhere.

KEY SCHEDULE
------------
`expand_key` is the FIPS-197 Sec.5.2 expansion. The RTL instead computes it ON THE FLY with one
128-bit round-key register, advancing once per round; `next_round_key` is that exact step
(w0' = w0 ^ SubWord(RotWord(w3)) ^ {rcon,24'h0}, w1' = w0'^w1, w2' = w1'^w2, w3' = w2'^w3, with
rcon advanced by `xtime` per round, starting at 0x01). selftest() proves the two formulations
agree, so a divergence between the RTL's on-the-fly schedule and the standard is attributable.

MIXCOLUMNS FORMULATION
----------------------
`mix_column` uses the same identity the RTL does,
b_i = a_i ^ t ^ xtime(a_i ^ a_(i+1 mod 4)),   t = a0^a1^a2^a3
which equals the textbook 2*a_i ^ 3*a_(i+1) ^ a_(i+2) ^ a_(i+3) (selftest() checks the two against
each other and against FIPS-197-style column vectors).

VECTORS PINNED HERE AND AGAIN IN THE SUITE
------------------------------------------
Each constant below is asserted by selftest() AND re-asserted, hardcoded independently, by
tb/cocotb/soc/test_crypto.py (the double-pin tb/models/trng_lfsr_model.py / test_trng.py
established), so a silent edit of this model cannot move the target the suite chases:
* FIPS-197 Appendix B   -- the worked example (key 2b7e1516..., plaintext 3243f6a8...).
* FIPS-197 Appendix C.1 -- the AES-128 known-answer vector (key 000102...0f).
* NIST SP 800-38A F.5.1 -- CTR-AES128 over four blocks (counter f0f1...feff).

Run `python3 tb/models/aes128_model.py` to execute the self-check.
\end{textblock}

\end{relocated}

\section{\texttt{tb/models/npu\_model.py}}\label{tm:suite:npu-model}

This section holds comment text that was in the source file. The text is unchanged. Each line has no leading comment marker and no indentation.

\begin{relocated}{tb/models/npu_model.py}

\subsection*{Module docstring}

\begin{textblock}
npu_model.py -- pure-Python golden model of the INT8 NPU's arithmetic (rtl/npu/npu_top.sv +
rtl/npu/npu_mac_array.sv + rtl/npu/npu_weight_mem.sv; bead claude_verilog_test-f7vs.11, Phase 6c,
Doc: ds:ch:phase6-ip-expansion-plan Sec.7 "6c -- NPU").
\end{textblock}

\subsection*{Before: from \_\_future\_\_ import annotations}

\begin{textblock}
STANDARD LIBRARY ONLY. requirements.txt is deliberately untouched (same promise as
tb/models/aes128_model.py): this is a test oracle, not a fast implementation.

This module is the authoritative specification of what the RTL must compute; the RTL implements
what this file computes. It models ONLY THE ARITHMETIC -- not cycle timing, not the register bank,
not the APB handshake, not the AIN / AOUT FIFO depths or the weight-SRAM read latency
(tb/cocotb/soc/test_npu.py pins all of that).

WHAT IS COMPUTED
----------------
For one inference of KLEN chunks (c = 0 .. KLEN-1) and four output lanes (j = 0 .. 3):

acc[j] = SUM_c SUM_(i=0..3)  W[c][i][j] * a[c][i]                  (signed INT32)
y[j]   = relu?( sat_int8( (acc[j] * SCALE_MULT) >> SCALE_SHIFT ) )

One chunk is one 32-bit AIN word carrying a[c][0..3] and four CONSECUTIVE weight-SRAM words
carrying W[c][i][0..3], one word per row i, at SRAM[TILEBASE + 4*c + i]. The four accumulators are
zeroed at START and accumulate across all KLEN chunks; the requantiser then drains the four lanes
and the four INT8 results are packed into ONE 32-bit AOUT word.

BYTE-LANE CONTRACT (one rule, used for WDATA, AIN and AOUT alike)
-----------------------------------------------------------------
LITTLE-ENDIAN: element / lane index 0 is bits [7:0], index 1 is [15:8], index 2 is [23:16] and
index 3 is [31:24] of the 32-bit register word.

word = (e3 & 0xFF) << 24 | (e2 & 0xFF) << 16 | (e1 & 0xFF) << 8 | (e0 & 0xFF)

So a weight word is row i of the tile with W[c][i][j] in byte j; an AIN word is a[c][0..3] with
a[c][i] in byte i; an AOUT word is y[0..3] with y[j] in byte j. Each byte is a two's-complement
INT8. Rationale: RV32 is little-endian, so firmware can `memcpy` an `int8_t[4]` straight into a
register with no byte swap (the opposite choice to the AES model, whose FIPS hex notation forced
big-endian). `pack_word` / `unpack_word` are the only place the conversion lives.

WEIGHT / ACTIVATION / PRODUCT RANGES
------------------------------------
W and a are signed INT8 (-128 .. 127). A product is INT8 x INT8 -> INT16 (-16256 .. 16384). Four
products per chunk, KLEN chunks per inference.

ACCUMULATOR: SIGNED INT32, SATURATING -- and the saturation can never fire
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
The accumulator policy is SATURATE (`sat_int32`), not wrap. But the reachable range is tiny: the
worst case is every operand -128 (product +16384), 4 products per chunk and 64 chunks, i.e.
256 * 16384 = 2**22 = 4_194_304, which is 2**9 times smaller than INT32_MAX. So for every legal
(KLEN <= 64) inference the INT32 accumulator CANNOT overflow, and `sat_int32` is the DEFINED
behaviour for a width the architecture never reaches. Consequences:
* the RTL is free to use a narrower accumulator (>= 24 bits signed holds every reachable value)
or the full 32 bits; the two are observably identical;
* what the RTL must NOT do is truncate or saturate the accumulator x scale PRODUCT to 32 bits
(see below) -- that product needs at least 40 bits and is a real, reachable overflow site.
`accumulate` applies `sat_int32` after every addition so the policy is executable and tested at the
INT32 limits (tb/tests/test_npu_model.py), even though no legal input reaches them.

REQUANTISE (the shared stage)
-----------------------------
SCALE register: SCALE[15:0] = SCALE_MULT (UNSIGNED 16-bit multiplier), SCALE[20:16] = SCALE_SHIFT
(unsigned right shift, 0 .. 31).

p  = acc * SCALE_MULT            exact signed product; NEVER truncated
s  = p >> SCALE_SHIFT            ARITHMETIC right shift = FLOOR division by 2**SCALE_SHIFT
y  = sat_int8(s)                 clamp to [-128, 127], applied to the FULL-WIDTH shifted value
y  = max(0, y)   if CTRL[0] RELU_EN

ROUNDING IS TRUNCATION TO MINUS INFINITY (floor), NOT round-half-up and NOT toward zero:
( 3 * 1) >> 1 =  1      (round-half-up would give 2)
(-1 * 1) >> 1 = -1      (toward-zero would give 0)
(-3 * 1) >> 1 = -2      (toward-zero and half-up would both give -1)
In RTL this is `$signed(prod) >>> shift`, with the product held wide enough for every reachable
value (|acc| <= 2**22 needs 24 signed bits, so 24 + 16 = 40 bits; a full-INT32 accumulator would
need 48) and the saturation compare done on the shifted full-width value. A 32-bit product
would wrap for e.g. acc = 2**22, mult = 65535, shift = 0: the true result saturates to 127,
a 32-bit wrap gives -128.
SCALE_MULT = 0 gives y = 0 for every lane. ReLU is applied AFTER saturation; since saturation is
monotone and ReLU is max(0, .), the order does not change the result, but this is the order the
RTL's requantiser implements.

ILLEGAL STARTS
--------------
`legal_start` is the legality predicate the RTL's 2-cycle zero-length path uses:
KLEN in 1 .. 63 and TILEBASE + 4*KLEN <= WEIGHT_WORDS (1024). `infer` refuses an illegal start
(ValueError) because the RTL computes nothing for one.

KLEN RANGE: 63 AT THE REGISTER, 64 IN THE MODEL (a plan defect, flagged)
-------------------------------------------------------------------------
The plan documents KLEN[5:0] as "1..64" with KLEN == 0 illegal. A 6-bit field cannot hold 64:
writing 64 stores 0 (WMASK 0x3F), which is then an illegal start. The register-reachable maximum
is therefore 63. The ARITHMETIC is defined here for 1 .. KLEN_MAX = 64 because the accumulator
bound above is stated for the plan's 64; `legal_start` enforces the register's real limit
(KLEN_REG_MAX = 63). Resolving the discrepancy (KLEN-1 encoding, or "max 63") belongs to the
plan's owner; the model needs no change either way for 1 .. 63.

Run `python3 tb/models/npu_model.py` to execute the self-check.
\end{textblock}

\end{relocated}

\section{\texttt{tb/models/soc\_model.py}}\label{tm:suite:soc-model}

This section holds comment text that was in the source file. The text is unchanged. Each line has no leading comment marker and no indentation.

\begin{relocated}{tb/models/soc_model.py}

\subsection*{Module docstring}

\begin{textblock}
soc_model.py — Phase 5 (M9) SoC reference model.

Composes existing RV32IModel (CPU golden) and MemoryModel (sparse byte
memory) into a SoC-level golden model for the boot test scoreboard.

Architecture:
ROM  : 0x0000_1000 – 0x0000_1FFF  (4 KB, read-only, loaded from hex file)
SRAM : 0x0000_2000 – 0x0FFF_FFFF  (sparse write-back, initially empty)
Other: DECERR (not modelled; CPU should not reach these in the boot test)

Reset PC override:
The real CPU resets at 0x0000_0000, but after the RESET_PC RTL fix it
resets at 0x0000_1000 (ROM_BASE).  This model always starts at ROM_BASE
so the golden trace is correct for the fixed RTL.

Usage::

model = SoCModel("path/to/boot.hex")
model.boot()
pcs  = model.committed_pcs    # list[int] — PCs in commit order
state = model.final_state()   # dict with registers + SRAM snapshot

Public API:
SoCModel(hex_path, rom_base, sram_base, sram_size)
.boot(max_insns)     — run until EBREAK or max_insns
.committed_pcs       — list of committed PCs (in order)
.final_state()       — dict: regs, sram
.sram_word(addr)     — read a 32-bit word from SRAM model
\end{textblock}

\end{relocated}

\section{\texttt{tb/models/trng\_lfsr\_model.py}}\label{tm:suite:trng-lfsr-model}

This section holds comment text that was in the source file. The text is unchanged. Each line has no leading comment marker and no indentation.

\begin{relocated}{tb/models/trng_lfsr_model.py}

\subsection*{Module docstring}

\begin{textblock}
trng_lfsr_model.py -- bit-exact pure-Python golden model of the TRNG's default (LFSR) entropy
source, rtl/periph/trng_lfsr_entropy.sv (bead claude_verilog_test-f7vs.8, Phase 6a-4,
Doc: ds:ch:phase6-ip-expansion-plan Sec.7 "6a-4 -- TRNG").
\end{textblock}

\subsection*{Before: from \_\_future\_\_ import annotations}

\begin{textblock}
THIS IS NOT A RANDOMNESS MODEL. The default-build TRNG is a deterministic pseudo-random generator
that advertises the fact by forcing TRNG_STATUS.INSECURE = 1. This module exists so the L1 suite
(tb/cocotb/soc/test_trng.py) can golden-vector the RTL: the whole point of the LFSR arm is that,
given TRNG_SEED, every word the peripheral ever produces is a pure function of the seed. It is the
authoritative specification of that function; the RTL implements what this file computes.

Entropy pipeline contract (one enabled, non-stalled core clock == one raw sample):

1. SEED MAPPING. Three Fibonacci LFSRs are loaded from the 32-bit TRNG_SEED by plain slicing:
lfsr31 = seed[30:0]     lfsr29 = seed[28:0]     lfsr23 = seed[22:0]
Seed bit 31 is unused. A slice of all zeros is a lock-up state (an LFSR loaded with 0 emits
0 forever); that is deliberately NOT rescued -- see TRNG_SEED == 0 below.
2. LFSR STEP. Register width n, tap t (1-based bit numbers, MSB = bit n):
out  = state[n-1]                          (the bit leaving the register)
fb   = state[n-1] ^ state[t-1]
state = ((state << 1) | fb) & (2**n - 1)
Polynomials (all primitive trinomials, maximal length 2**n - 1; verified by selftest()):
31-bit  x^31 + x^28 + 1  taps (31, 28)
29-bit  x^29 + x^27 + 1  taps (29, 27)
23-bit  x^23 + x^18 + 1  taps (23, 18)
The recurrence this realises is out[k] = out[k-n] ^ out[k-t], i.e. the trinomial
x^n + x^(n-t) + 1 -- the reciprocal of the well-known x^n + x^t + 1 form, also primitive.
3. RAW SAMPLE. raw[k] = out31[k] ^ out29[k] ^ out23[k]; all three registers step once per raw
sample. Sample 0 is taken from the freshly loaded state (before any step).
4. VON NEUMANN DEBIASER over consecutive NON-overlapping raw pairs (raw[2j], raw[2j+1]):
01 -> emit 0,  10 -> emit 1  (i.e. emit the FIRST bit of the pair),  00 / 11 -> discard.
For unbiased independent input 50 % of pairs survive, so ~4 raw samples buy 1 output bit.
5. WORD ASSEMBLY. Output bits are packed LSB-first: the first emitted bit is word bit 0, the
32nd is bit 31. A completed word is pushed to the FIFO; TRNG_DATA pops them in that order.
6. BACKPRESSURE IS A STALL, NOT A DROP. When a completed word cannot enter the (4-deep) FIFO,
the pipeline stops consuming raw samples (LFSRs hold). No sample is ever discarded for lack
of FIFO space, so the SEQUENCE of words popped is a function of the seed alone -- it does not
depend on when, or how fast, software reads.
7. HEALTH TEST (NIST SP 800-90B Sec.4.4.1 Repetition Count Test) on the RAW samples (the noise-
source output, before debiasing): run length of identical consecutive raw samples;
reaching CUTOFF = 21 (H = 1 bit/sample, alpha = 2**-20 -> C = 1 + ceil(20/1)) is a failure.
The count includes the first sample of a run (a run of 21 identical samples trips).
7b. After a health trip the pipeline HALTS (no further raw samples consumed) and the FIFO is
flushed; a W1C of STATUS.health_fail resumes it from the current LFSR state with the run
length restarted from empty (a still-stuck source therefore re-trips 21 samples later).
8. SESSION. The LFSRs load from TRNG_SEED, and the FIFO / word assembler / health run counter
are cleared, on every 0 -> 1 edge of CTRL.ENABLE. TRNG_SEED writes at any other time only
update the register (they take effect at the next enable edge).

TRNG_SEED == 0 (or any seed whose low 31 bits are 0) therefore loads all three LFSRs with 0: the
raw stream is constant 0, no pair ever survives the debiaser (no word is ever produced) and the
repetition-count test trips at raw sample 21. That is the intended behaviour -- a dead source is
reported (STATUS.health_fail), not silently papered over.

Run `python3 tb/models/trng_lfsr_model.py` to execute the self-check (primitivity of the three
polynomials, hand-checked first steps, and the known-answer words for the reset seed).
\end{textblock}

\end{relocated}

\section{\texttt{tb/cocotb/bfm/i2c\_slave.py}}\label{tm:suite:bfm-i2c-slave}

This section holds comment text that was in the source file. The text is unchanged. Each line has no leading comment marker and no indentation.

\begin{relocated}{tb/cocotb/bfm/i2c_slave.py}

\subsection*{Module docstring}

\begin{textblock}
I2C Slave / Bus BFM (Bus Functional Model)

Models the PHYSICAL I2C BUS and one register-file slave sitting on it, for testbenches that drive
an open-drain I2C *master* (rtl/periph/i2c_controller.sv).  Companion to apb4_master.py: that BFM
speaks the register bus, this one speaks the pins.

Signals sampled (DUT master outputs, open-drain triplets, `<prefix>` is "i2c_" in tb_i2c /
tb_soc_top):
<prefix>scl_oe_o, <prefix>scl_o, <prefix>sda_oe_o, <prefix>sda_o
Signals driven (DUT master inputs, the sensed pad levels):
<prefix>scl_i, <prefix>sda_i

The wired-AND lives HERE, not in the RTL
----------------------------------------
The DUT's pad-ring contract is: oe = 1 drives the line LOW, oe = 0 releases it, an external pull-up
makes a released line high, and `*_o` is a dead hard-tied-0 pin that exists only for pad symmetry
with GPIO.  The bus is therefore reconstructed in the testbench, every clock:

sda_bus = (dut.i2c_sda_oe_o ? dut.i2c_sda_o : 1) & slave_drive & ~forced_low
scl_bus = (dut.i2c_scl_oe_o ? dut.i2c_scl_o : 1) & slave_drive & ~forced_low

and driven back into dut.i2c_sda_i / dut.i2c_scl_i.  Because the BFM is the only thing that closes
the loop, clock stretching (the slave holds SCL low), arbitration loss (another master holds SDA
low while this master releases it) and a stuck bus are all just "slave_drive" / "forced_low"
values -- no RTL hook is needed or used.  The BFM also asserts, every tick, that the DUT's dead
`*_o` pins read 0 (`protocol_errors`), so a regression that starts using `*_o` as data is caught.

!!! TRAP (already hit once, do not rediscover): DRIVE THE BUS ON THE NEGEDGE !!!
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The DUT samples `i2c_scl_i` / `i2c_sda_i` (through its 2-FF synchronisers) and registers its own
oe outputs on the clock's RISING edge.  A BFM that reads the oe pins and writes the `_i` pins in
the SAME rising-edge callback races the DUT: cocotb/Verilator then hands the DUT either the old or
the new bus level depending on scheduling order, and the failure looks exactly like an RTL fault
-- during the RTL bring-up it produced two spurious read-back failures that were not RTL faults.
This BFM therefore does ALL of its reading and driving on the FALLING edge of the clock, half a
period away from every DUT sampling edge.  Every `_i` value the DUT sees is stable for a whole
clock before it is sampled, and every oe value this BFM reads has fully settled after the
preceding rising edge.  Do not "optimise" this to RisingEdge.  The consequence is that the slave
reacts with zero delay at the sampling tick, which is indistinguishable (to a master whose tick is
>= 4 clk) from a real slave that responds inside the SCL-low interval.

Slave behaviour
---------------
One slave at `address` (7-bit), register-file style (like an EEPROM / sensor):
* address phase: ACK iff the 7-bit address matches and `ack_address` is set, else NACK and the
slave ignores the transaction until the next START/STOP;
* write: the FIRST data byte after an address-write is the register pointer (not stored); every
later byte is stored at mem[ptr++].  `data_acks` is a per-byte ACK(True)/NACK(False) script
consumed in order for write DATA bytes (exhausted = ACK).  A NACKed byte is not stored;
* read: returns `read_queue` bytes first (if any), otherwise mem[ptr++]; stops driving after the
master NACKs.
Everything the slave observes is appended to `events`:
("START",)  ("RSTART",)  ("ADDR", addr7, rw, acked)  ("WR", byte, acked)
("RD", byte, master_acked)  ("STOP",)
A START with no preceding STOP is logged as RSTART (repeated START), which is how a test tells a
genuine repeated START from a STOP followed by a START.

Hooks (all take effect on the next negedge; all are optional)
-------------------------------------------------------------
stretch_at_fall(index, cycles)  hold SCL low for `cycles` clk right after SCL FALL number `index`
stretch_every_fall(cycles)      same, after every SCL fall
hold_scl_low(flag)              hold SCL low until released (stuck / dead slave)
contend_at_fall(index, cycles)  drive SDA low for `cycles` clk right after SCL fall `index` --
a second master driving a 0 while this master sends a 1
hold_sda_low(flag)              hold SDA low until released (bus busy / stuck SDA)
SCL fall numbering: the count restarts at every START/repeated START; the master's pull-down right
after the START condition is fall 0, and the next SCL-high pulse is bit `index`.  So for byte
number n (0 = the address byte) bit i (0 = MSB) the preceding fall is 9*n + i, and byte n's ACK slot
is 9*n + 8.

Built-in checks (collected, not raised, so a test decides)
----------------------------------------------------------
protocol_errors  the master advanced (moved SDA, or pulled SCL low again) while the slave was
stretching SCL and the master had released it -- i.e. it did not honour clock
stretching; or a dead `*_o` pin read 1;
abandoned        a START/STOP that arrived mid-byte (bit count in parentheses) -- a master that
lost arbitration or timed out walks away mid-byte, so this is informational
there and a hard error everywhere else (assert_clean() checks both).
Timing records for rate checks: scl_rise_cycles / scl_fall_cycles (BFM clock-tick timestamps of
every SCL edge on the bus), plus `scl_low_cycles` / `scl_high_cycles` / `scl_periods`.
\end{textblock}

\end{relocated}

\section{\texttt{tb/cocotb/common/sim\_portability.mk}}\label{tm:suite:common-sim-portability-mk}

This section holds comment text that was in the source file. The text is unchanged. Each line has no leading comment marker and no indentation.

\begin{relocated}{tb/cocotb/common/sim_portability.mk}

\subsection*{Before: ifeq (\$(SIM),verilator)}

\begin{textblock}
sim_portability.mk -- let the cocotb Makefiles in this repo drive Synopsys VCS and Cadence
Xcelium (xrun) as well as Verilator.

Included by tb/cocotb/soc/Makefile, tb/cocotb/cpu/Makefile and sim/Makefile, immediately BEFORE
their `include $(shell cocotb-config --makefiles)/Makefile.sim`.  It only defines variables (no
targets, so it can never change a Makefile's default goal).

INVARIANT: with SIM=verilator (the default, and what CI runs) this file changes nothing the
including Makefile does.  It defines one identity helper (`sp_lint`) and leaves every other
variable alone; `make -n` output of every target is byte-identical with and without the include
(checked with `cmp -s`, see the commit that introduced this file).

Inputs the including Makefile may set BEFORE the include (all optional):
  SIM_PORT_KEYED_BUILD=1  give each distinct build recipe its own SIM_BUILD dir, keyed by content
                          (the soc Makefile's scheme; the Verilator key lives in that Makefile).
  SIM_PORT_DEFINES        `+define+` names that the Makefile adds only inside its `ifeq ($(SIM),verilator)`
                          block but that the RTL needs under every simulator (e.g. SIMULATION).
Inputs from the caller's command line:
  SIM_RESULTS_IN_BUILD=1  write cocotb's results.xml to $(SIM_BUILD)/results__<MODULE>.xml instead of the
                          shared ./results.xml.  Needed to run several targets of one directory
                          concurrently, and to keep each build point's verdict (several soc targets
                          run two build points back to back; ./results.xml keeps only the last).
  VCS_LINT_FLAGS          default +lint=TFIPC-L (port-connection width/type mismatches become warnings).
  VCS_EXTRA_COMPILE       extra raw vcs compile flags (e.g. -kdb for Verdi).

What it does when SIM is vcs or xcelium
---------------------------------------
 * EXTRA_ARGS and COMPILE_ARGS are translated, never forwarded raw (VCS and xrun reject or silently
   misread most Verilator flags):
       -G<P>=<v>        -> VCS   -pvalue+<TOPLEVEL>.<P>=<v>      Xcelium  -defparam <TOPLEVEL>.<P>=<v>
                           (a quoted string value such as -GMEM_INIT_FILE=\"path\" is kept as written)
       -D<X> / +define+ -> +define+<X>                          (native to both)
       -I<d> / +incdir+ -> +incdir+<d>                          (native to both)
       -W*  --*  -O*  -j*  and the value-taking Verilator flags (-CFLAGS, --output-split, ...)
                        -> dropped (Verilator-only: lint switches, tracing, coverage, optimisation)
       anything else (e.g. `-y dir`, a native +plusarg) -> passed through untouched.
   The result goes into COMPILE_ARGS.  EXTRA_ARGS is UNDEFINED afterwards: it arrives from the sub-make
   command line, where cocotb's own `EXTRA_ARGS += ...` (VCS: -full64, Xcelium: the whole
   -top/-xmlibdirpath/-licqueue block) would otherwise be silently ignored, and cocotb's VCS rule
   also hands EXTRA_ARGS to the simv run line, where -pvalue/-defparam do not belong.
 * Xcelium gets -nocopyright, -nohistory and a per-build log (-l $(SIM_BUILD)/xrun.log) so concurrent
   builds in one directory do not trample xrun.log / xrun.history.
 * `$(call sp_lint,<lint targets>)` is empty, so a `foo: foo_lint` prerequisite becomes a no-op:
   Verilator --lint-only has no VCS/xrun equivalent run here; compile cleanliness is judged from the
   VCS/xrun compile logs instead (every warning triaged by hand, see the bring-up report).
 * COVERAGE=1: SIM=vcs instruments with `-cm line+cond+fsm+tgl+branch+assert` (section "VCS code
   coverage" at the end of this file); SIM=xcelium REFUSES it (no imc on the host to merge/report
   with -- a collected-but-unreportable database is worse than a clear error).
 * SIM_BUILD is keyed by SIM so Verilator, VCS and Xcelium objects can never collide.
\end{textblock}

\subsection*{Before: define sp\_nl}

\begin{textblock}
Scope = the rtl/ files this build actually compiles: its VERILOG_SOURCES plus whatever the -I/-y
library lookup can pull in (a module found only through -y, e.g. rv32i_clock_gate.sv, is RTL too).
Testbench wrappers (tb_*), cocotb glue and the behavioural simulation models under sim/ are not
listed, so they are excluded at COMPILE time (a leading +filelist rule excludes everything else),
not by filtering the report afterwards.  Listing only files this build uses keeps VCS from warning
(VCM-HFUFF) about every unused rtl/ file.  The list is content-addressed (name carries a hash of
it), so builds with different sources never share or race on a file.
\end{textblock}

\subsection*{Before: ifeq (\$(SIM)\$(if \$(COVERAGE),-cov),vcs-cov)}

\begin{textblock}
--- VCS code coverage (run side) ---------------------------------------------------------------
Every simulation runs with the SAME -cm metrics as the compile, into the build's own simv.vdb
(-cm_dir; the compile wrote the static design there) under a UNIQUE -cm_name:
    <build dir>__<MODULE>[__tc<TESTCASE hash>]__<hash of the abs build dir>
MODULE is in the name because suites that share a build dir (soc_integration_batch, the soc_top
chains of tools/eda/regress_specs.py) share one simv.vdb, and the hash of the build dir keeps names
unique when per-job vdbs from different SIM_BUILD_ROOTs are merged by one urg call.  Two simulations
with the same -cm_name in one vdb would OVERWRITE silently.  -cm_dir/-cm_name are run-only options, so
they go in SIM_ARGS (cocotb puts SIM_ARGS on the simv line only; PLUSARGS/EXTRA_ARGS also reach the
compile line).  Merge + report: tools/eda/vcs_coverage.py (urg).
\end{textblock}

\end{relocated}

\section{\texttt{tb/cocotb/cpu/legacy/test\_isa\_compliance.py}}\label{tm:suite:cpu-legacy-test-isa-compliance}

This section holds comment text that was in the source file. The text is unchanged. Each line has no leading comment marker and no indentation.

\begin{relocated}{tb/cocotb/cpu/legacy/test_isa_compliance.py}

\subsection*{Module docstring}

\begin{textblock}
ISA Compliance Tests for RV32I CPU - Phase 1

Tests all 37 RV32I base integer instructions in isolation to verify
ISA compliance. Each instruction is tested with various operands and
edge cases, with results validated against the Python reference model
via the scoreboard.

================================================================================
PYUVM MIGRATION WAIVER
================================================================================

STATUS: This test currently uses raw cocotb instead of pyuvm UVM infrastructure.

JUSTIFICATION:
Phase 1 verification plan (Doc: ds:ch:verification-plan) specifies
that tests should use pyuvm sequences and scoreboards for better methodology
and reusability. However, this test file contains 1875 lines of working,
validated ISA compliance tests that provide critical verification coverage.

Migrating to pyuvm would require:
1. Implementing uvm_sequence for instruction generation
2. Creating uvm_driver to interface with AXI/APB protocols
3. Implementing uvm_monitor to observe commit interface
4. Converting CPUScoreboard to uvm_scoreboard
5. Creating uvm_agent to encapsulate driver/monitor/sequencer
6. Building uvm_env with agent + scoreboard
7. Implementing uvm_test top-level
8. Re-validating all 37 instruction tests

This is a significant refactoring effort (estimated 3-5 days) with risk of
introducing regressions in working tests.

CURRENT APPROACH:
- Raw cocotb provides direct DUT access and fast test development
- CPUScoreboard validates commits against RV32IModel reference
- All 37 RV32I instructions have passing tests
- Tests are maintainable and well-documented

MIGRATION PLAN (DEFERRED TO PHASE 2):
When migrating to pyuvm (recommended for Phase 2+):
1. Create separate pyuvm-based test infrastructure in parallel
2. Implement UVM components (driver, monitor, agent, scoreboard)
3. Port individual tests incrementally, validating each
4. Keep raw cocotb tests as golden reference until migration complete
5. Remove cocotb version only after full pyuvm coverage verified

REFERENCES:
- Phase 1 Verification Plan: Doc: ds:ch:verification-plan
- pyuvm Documentation: https://github.com/pyuvm/pyuvm
- Current test coverage: All 37 RV32I instructions ✓

DECISION: Accept raw cocotb for Phase 1, plan pyuvm migration for Phase 2
DATE: 2026-01-30
APPROVED BY: Architecture review (deferred migration)
================================================================================
\end{textblock}

\end{relocated}

\begin{relocated}{tb/cocotb/cpu/test_csr_access.py}

\subsection*{Module docstring}

\begin{textblock}
CSR-file access coverage for rv32i_cpu_top (bead a5ze, Zicsr / Phase 5 M7).

Every test runs a generated CSR instruction sequence on the DUT and compares each rd with the
expected value from tb/models/csr_model.py, which is written from the project spec
(Doc: ds:ch:phase2-architecture 4.2, PHASE5 M7 counter map), not from the RTL.  The
GPR results are read back through the APB debug window, so no internal signal is touched.

test_mstatus_all_ops              CSRRW/RS/RC and the three immediate forms on mstatus, incl.
the rs1==x0 / uimm==0 write suppression and WARL masking
test_mie_mtvec_mepc_mcause_ops    the same op matrix on mie, mtvec (MODE forced to 0), mepc,
mcause, mcountinhibit (bit 1 and bits [31:6] read 0)
test_id_mip_and_readonly_csrs     mvendorid/marchid/mimpid/mhartid read values, mip following
timer_irq_i / ext_irq_i, writes to read-only CSRs ignored,
0x7C0 / 0x7C1 read back 0
test_counter_words_frozen         mcycle[h], minstret[h], mhpmcounter3-5 written and read back
with every op while mcountinhibit freezes them
test_counter_carry_into_high_word mcycle / minstret low-word wrap carries into the high word
test_dcache_flush_op_forms        0x7C0 fires only for non-suppressed forms (observed as a
write-back reaching the AXI memory)
test_dcache_inval_op_forms        0x7C1 likewise (observed as a dirty line being discarded)
test_mcycle_counts_every_clock_cycle           mcycle vs the testbench clock count (kiit)
test_counters_match_cycle_model_random         per-cycle counter state vs CsrModel.tick() over
random programs with events coinciding with CSRs
test_counter_carry_vs_write_alignment          low/high-word carry against a write to the other
half, swept over the alignment
test_counters_through_trap_and_mret            counters keep counting in trap-entry / MRET cycles
test_minstret_matches_retirements_between_reads  minstret delta == retire strobes between reads
\end{textblock}

in the else of "if (csr_access && !csr_illegal)", so every cycle a legal CSR instruction was in EX
dropped ALL increments.  Measured before the fix: 20 counted over 30 real cycles in a window of
10 CSR instructions.  The +-1 tolerance below must not be widened.

\section{\texttt{tb/cocotb/cpu/test\_hazard\_unit.py}}\label{tm:suite:cpu-test-hazard-unit}

This section holds comment text that was in the source file. The text is unchanged. Each line has no leading comment marker and no indentation.

\begin{relocated}{tb/cocotb/cpu/test_hazard_unit.py}

\subsection*{Module docstring}

\begin{textblock}
Standalone unit tests for rv32i_hazard_unit — GH #119 bead `gvr`.

The DUT (rtl/cpu/core/rv32i_hazard_unit.sv) is purely combinational: no clock,
no reset, no always_ff. Nothing in the repo drove it standalone before this
file (tb/cocotb/cpu/test_pipeline_hazards.py is full-core level).

Two layers, run as one cocotb test module:

(a) Directed tests — oracle is the DUT's header comment (lines 1-26 of the
RTL), NOT the implementation body. Expected stall/flush/forward values
below are derived by hand from that documented 9-item priority list and
the forwarding encoding. Where the header does not fully determine an
answer (see "SPEC AMBIGUITY" comments), the test asserts only the part
that IS determined, or is skipped.

(b) Randomised cross-arm differential (test_random_cross_arm) — dumps
(inputs, outputs) for N biased-random vectors to a JSON trace file
named after TOPLEVEL. tools/verif/compare_hazard_traces.py diffs two
such trace files (hand-RTL vs HLS arm) to provide the actual
cross-arm equivalence evidence; the directed tests above only check
one arm against the spec.

GH #119 decision: the two arms have genuinely different protocols (hand-RTL
is pure combinational; the Bambu HLS arm is wrapped in a clock/reset/
start_port/done_port handshake by construction). This file branches on that
difference in exactly one place -- HazardUnitDriver.__init__ / .apply() --
detected at runtime via hasattr(dut, "clk"). Every vector, every expected
value, and every assertion after that point is identical for both arms.
\end{textblock}

\subsection*{Before: INPUT\_FIELDS = [}

\begin{textblock}
All hand-RTL / HLS-shim input port names (identical on both arms per the
GH #119 shim contract -- the shim re-exposes the repo's port names verbatim
plus the HLS handshake).
\end{textblock}

\section{\texttt{tb/cocotb/cpu/test\_minstret\_write.py}}\label{tm:suite:cpu-test-minstret-write}

This section holds comment text that was in the source file. The text is unchanged. Each line has no leading comment marker and no indentation.

\begin{relocated}{tb/cocotb/cpu/test_minstret_write.py}

\subsection*{Module docstring}

\begin{textblock}
Directed tests for a CSR write to minstret / minstreth (bead f7z3).

RISC-V privileged spec: a write to minstret takes effect as if every older instruction had
already retired, and the writing instruction's own retirement does not increment the counter.  The
core applies the write in EX while the retirement strobe trails EX by five stages, so before the
fix older in-flight instructions and the writer itself retired on top of the written value.

Expected values come from the spec, not from the RTL:

* ``V <= minstret read <= V + n`` where ``n`` is the number of instructions that retired between
the writer and the reader (read from the commit stream), and the read is exactly ``V`` when the
reader directly follows the writer (``n == 0``);
* a read may trail by at most ONE retirement (the reader's immediate predecessor has not
retired yet when the reader executes in EX).  That read lag is separate from f7z3 and is pinned
by ``test_minstret_matches_retirements_between_reads``; it is allowed for here, never more;
* every test also runs the per-cycle ``CounterMonitor`` against ``CsrModel.tick()``, which models
the in-flight absorption by program-order index (tb/models/csr_model.py).

test_write_read_back_at_distance          write, then a read 0..3 instructions later, at four
instruction-cache alignments
test_write_with_older_instruction_in_flight  ALU / CSR / load hit / load miss / load-use / taken
branch in front of the write, and a branch behind it
test_zeroing_both_halves_back_to_back     csrw minstret ; csrw minstreth reads (0, 0)
test_low_half_wrap_carries_only_younger_retirements   write 0xFFFFFFFF, no spurious carry
test_high_half_write_keeps_counting_the_low_half
test_read_only_forms_are_not_writes       CSRRS/CSRRC rs1=x0, CSRRSI/CSRRCI uimm=0
test_set_clear_forms_write_with_nonzero_source
test_wrong_path_write_is_ignored          CSR writes in a taken branch / JAL shadow
test_write_with_older_trap_taken_while_in_flight   writer in flight across an exception
test_mcycle_write_wins_and_counts_on      mcycle / mcycleh analysis (no in-flight term)
test_hpm_event_of_older_load_lands_after_write   mhpmcounter4 analysis (time-ordered event)
\end{textblock}

\end{relocated}

\section{\texttt{tb/cocotb/cpu/test\_perf\_counters.py}}\label{tm:suite:cpu-test-perf-counters}

This section holds comment text that was in the source file. The text is unchanged. Each line has no leading comment marker and no indentation.

\begin{relocated}{tb/cocotb/cpu/test_perf_counters.py}

\subsection*{Module docstring}

\begin{textblock}
Phase 5 M7 — Performance counter directed tests for rv32i_cpu_top.

Subtests:
a. test_a_mcycle_increments      — mcycle advances each cycle; frozen with mcountinhibit[0]
b. test_b_minstret_counts_retires— minstret increments once per committed instruction
c. test_c_mcountinhibit_freeze   — inhibit[0] freezes mcycle; inhibit[2] freezes minstret
d. test_d_hpm3_icache_miss       — mhpmcounter3 counts I-cache misses (cold-start misses)
e. test_e_hpm4_dcache_miss       — mhpmcounter4 counts D-cache misses (cold-start load misses)
f. test_f_hpm5_branch_mispred    — mhpmcounter5 counts branch mispredictions
g. test_g_mimpid_phase5          — mimpid reads 0x00000005 (Phase 5 implementation ID)

Observability: all counter values are captured into GPRs via CSRRS, then read back through
the APB debug window (0x010 + reg*4). No internal RTL signals are accessed directly.

Setup strategy: each test calls _setup_perf_test(dut, program) which:
1. Starts the 10 ns clock
2. Initialises DUT inputs (clears stale AXI/APB/IRQ values)
3. Creates ConfigurableAXIMemory (starts AXI handlers)
4. Writes the program into the AXI memory dict before reset fires
— this guarantees the CPU fetches valid instructions from address 0x0000
even for the very first fetch after reset deasserts.
5. Resets the DUT (rst_n_i low for 10 cycles, then deasserts)
6. Returns (mem, dbg) for use by the caller.

This is the only difference from phase2_test_utils._setup_test: program is in memory
before reset, so no race between reset deassert and first fetch.
\end{textblock}

\end{relocated}

\section{\texttt{tb/cocotb/gpu/gpu\_ref\_model.py}}\label{tm:suite:gpu-gpu-ref-model}

This section holds comment text that was in the source file. The text is unchanged. Each line has no leading comment marker and no indentation.

\begin{relocated}{tb/cocotb/gpu/gpu_ref_model.py}

\subsection*{Module docstring}

\begin{textblock}
Lightweight reference interpreter for the Phase 4 GPU ISA — matches the actual
RTL/gpu_asm opcode encoding (gpu_pkg.sv), NOT tb/models/gpu_kernel_model.py
(which uses a different, RISC-V-style encoding and has no shared-mem ops).

Scope: single warp of N_LANES lanes, straight-line and single-level-divergent
execution (ALU R/I-type, VMOV_TID_X/BID_X, VLD/VST global, VLDS/VSTS shared,
VSYNC barrier, VRET terminate, and VBLT/VBGE/VBEQ/VBNE/VJMP branch opcodes for
single-level SIMT divergence).

SIMT divergence semantics (single-level stack, matches gpu_compute_unit.sv):
- Branch: split lanes into taken/not-taken masks.
* If both masks non-zero: divergent → push (reconverge_pc=fall_through_pc+branch,
false_mask=not_taken) onto stack; execute true-path lanes (taken mask).
* If only taken: converging taken → next_pc = branch_target, same mask.
* If only not-taken: converging not-taken → next_pc = fall-through (pc+4).
- VRET with non-empty stack → pop stack; continue at (return_pc, return_mask)
(implements false-path execution after true-path VRET).
- VRET with empty stack → terminate.
- VJMP: unconditional PC-relative jump (all active lanes).

Random generator only emits guaranteed-reconverging single-level diamond patterns
and modeled subset ops, so any mismatch is a real datapath bug.

Launch contract (bead 6hy6, user decision 2026-10-11) -- block-level helpers below the class:
- GPU_BLOCK_X must be 1..MAX_BLOCK_THREADS (64 = N_WARPS x N_LANES).  0 or > 64 is a launch
ERROR (GPU_STATUS reads ERROR, no kernel instruction executes): ``launch_is_error``.
- A block of BLOCK_X threads runs ceil(BLOCK_X / N_LANES) warps; the last warp starts with
ONLY its valid lanes active (BLOCK_X=9: warp 0 all 8 lanes, warp 1 lane 0 only):
``warp_lane_mask`` -> ``GpuRefModel(init_mask=...)``.  Invalid lanes never write registers,
shared memory or global memory.
\end{textblock}

\end{relocated}

\section{\texttt{tb/cocotb/gpu/kernels/kernel\_block\_warps.py}}\label{tm:suite:gpu-kernels-kernel-block-warps}

This section holds comment text that was in the source file. The text is unchanged. Each line has no leading comment marker and no indentation.

\begin{relocated}{tb/cocotb/gpu/kernels/kernel_block_warps.py}

\subsection*{Module docstring}

\begin{textblock}
Kernel test: GPU_BLOCK_X launch contract (beads a5ze, 47lf, 6hy6).

The architecture is 8 warps x 8 lanes = 64 threads (CLAUDE.md "8 warps max; 64 total threads",
PHASE4 spec "Warp size: 8 lanes").  Contract, user decision 2026-10-11 (bead 6hy6):

* BLOCK_X in 1..64 runs ceil(BLOCK_X / 8) warps and EXACTLY BLOCK_X threads take effect:
the last warp starts with only its valid lanes active (BLOCK_X=9: warp 0 all 8 lanes,
warp 1 lane 0 only).  An invalid lane never writes a register, shared memory or AXI.
* BLOCK_X == 0 or BLOCK_X > 64 raises the GPU ERROR condition at launch: no hang, no silent
truncation, no kernel instruction executes.  It reuses the q6w0 ERROR path: GPU_STATUS
reads ERROR, the GPU is halted, no done IRQ, and CTRL.reset then a legal launch recovers.

Marker kernel (every warp writes warp_id + 1 to its own word):

vmov_bid_x r2           r2[l] = warp_id
vaddi r7, r2, 1         r7[l] = warp_id + 1          (marker, never 0)
vaddi r3, r0, 2
vsll  r4, r2, r3        r4[l] = warp_id * 4
vaddi r9, r4, OUT-7     r9[l] = OUT + warp_id*4 - 7
vst   r7 -> r9 + 7      mem[OUT + warp_id*4] = warp_id + 1
vret

Tests:

test_block_sizes_below_cap       BLOCK_X = 1, 8, 9, 16, 17, 55, 56: ceil(BLOCK_X / 8) warps
test_block_sizes_57_to_64        EVERY BLOCK_X in 57..64 runs all 8 warps (bead 47lf / GH #254:
the warp COUNT was held in 3 bits and saturated at 7)
test_lane_results_match_model    a per-LANE kernel; for every legal BLOCK_X the complete set of
stores equals the GpuRefModel union with per-warp lane masks:
no missing store, no extra store (one AXI write per thread,
counted), no wrong value
test_invalid_lanes_have_no_side_effects   register-file, shared-memory and global-memory write
strobes are checked every cycle against the valid-lane map,
and the surviving register / shared-memory state is read back
by a follow-up 64-thread kernel (partial warp leaves the
previous kernel's values in the invalid lanes)
test_partial_warp_divergence     a divergent branch counts only valid lanes: BLOCK_X=9 pushes
one divergence entry (warp 0), not two
test_illegal_block_x_raises_error  BLOCK_X in 0, 65, 72, 128, 1023: STATUS reads ERROR (only)
within a bounded time, nothing is fetched or stored, no IRQ,
then CTRL.reset + a legal launch gives the right results
test_illegal_block_x_after_done  the same from DONE (launch without reset), and 64 -> OK
\end{textblock}

\end{relocated}

\section{\texttt{tb/cocotb/gpu/kernels/kernel\_control\_paths.py}}\label{tm:suite:gpu-kernels-kernel-control-paths}

\begin{relocated}{tb/cocotb/gpu/kernels/kernel_control_paths.py}

\subsection*{Module docstring}

\begin{textblock}
gpu_top control-plane and error paths (bead a5ze, slice 2).

DUT = gpu_top.  What each test pins (spec: Doc: ds:ch:phase4-gpu-architecture "Control Interface":
GPU_CTRL = start / reset, GPU_STATUS = idle / done / error; "nested branches NOT supported in
Phase 4 (kernel must avoid)" with a 4-deep per-warp divergence stack, gpu_pkg::DIV_STACK_DEPTH):

test_register_file_paths      BLOCK_Y / BLOCK_Z / IRQ_STATUS read back; writes to unmapped
offsets change nothing and unmapped reads return 0
test_done_returns_to_idle     a finished kernel (STATUS.done) goes back to idle on a second
CTRL.launch, and on CTRL.reset; the IRQ latch clears on reset
test_four_deep_nesting_ok     four nested divergent branches fit the stack and finish
test_stack_overflow_sets_error   a fifth nested divergence overflows the stack: STATUS.error,

test_soft_reset_recovers_from_error   CTRL.reset leaves ERROR for idle and a fresh kernel then
runs to the correct result
test_error_halts_then_reset_then_divergent_kernel_matches_model   (bead q6w0) ERROR halts the
