\subsection{Ranked hole list (highest risk first)}\label{tm:flow:commercial-sim-coverage:3-ranked-hole-list-highest-risk-first}\origsec{3}

Ranking key: (1) unvisited FSM states and transitions, (2) error /
\code{default} / DECERR / SLVERR paths, (3) reset, flush and abort
paths, (4) CDC and async FIFO corner cases, (5) PMU timeout, (6) crypto
illegal start, (7) NPU KLEN extremes, (8) DMA error paths, (9) the rest.
Within a class, by consequence of a latent bug and then by size. The
user\textquotesingle s class order is kept, but note one measured fact
that reshapes it: \textbf{all FSM states were reached, and 58 of the 76
missing FSM transitions are the reset arm of the state register}
(\texttt{if\ (!rst\_\allowbreak{}n)\ state\ <=\allowbreak{}\ IDLE},
which VCS counts as an arc from every non-idle state). Classes 1 and 3
therefore overlap; they are listed once, under class 1, and tagged.

Evidence for classes the user asked about, even where the answer is "no
hole":

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{PMU timeout (\code{ISO_ACK_TIMEOUT}, \code{STATUS[3]})}: not a
  hole. \code{pmu.sv} has no uncovered line in the \code{g_ack_cpu} wait
  and timeout block; the timeout path is exercised (the suites written
  for it are \code{test_pmu.py} and \code{test_soc_pmu_iso_ack.py};
  per-test attribution was not extracted). The remaining \code{pmu}
  holes are H6 (reset during sequencing) and the waivable items W5.
\item
  \textbf{Crypto illegal start / \code{key_write_rejected}}: not a
  code-coverage hole. \code{crypto_accel} line 100 \%, branch 100 \%,
  FSM 100 \%; \code{aes128_core} line 100 \%. Condition rows are in
  section 6 (caveat C3).
\item
  \textbf{NPU KLEN extremes}: not visible to code coverage.
  \code{npu_top} line/\allowbreak{}cond/\allowbreak{}branch 100 \%, and
  no KLEN bit is among its never-toggled signals (every bit toggled both
  ways). Whether KLEN = 1 and KLEN = 63 are both run is a
  \emph{functional coverage} question; no covergroup exists for the NPU
  (section 7).
\item
  \textbf{CDC and async FIFO full/\allowbreak{}empty}:
  \code{cdc_gray_fifo} line/\allowbreak{}cond/\allowbreak{}branch 100
  \%, toggle 96.5 \% (the residual are payload bits), so full and empty
  are exercised. \code{async_axi_fifo} has no line/\allowbreak{}branch
  objects (it is structural) and cond 100 \%. The CDC holes are in
  \code{apb_cdc_bridge} (H7).
\item
  \textbf{DMA error paths}: error handling lines are covered
  (\code{dma_engine} line 98.7 \%, only L394 and a \code{default:}
  open). The DMA holes are queue depth and one dead branch (H9).
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{Top 15}\label{tm:flow:commercial-sim-coverage:31-top-15}\origsec{3.1}

H1 & FSM arcs, reset in flight & \code{axi4_crossbar.sv}
352/\allowbreak{}418/\allowbreak{}475 (12 arcs:
\code{W_AW},\code{W_DATA},\code{R_AR},\code{DW_DATA} to IDLE, per
slave/\allowbreak{}master); \code{axi_lite_interconnect.sv} 192/319 (3);
\code{axil_to_apb.sv} 185 (2); \code{axi4_to_axilite.sv} 160 (2);
\code{boot_rom.sv} 144 (1); \code{sram_controller.sv} 241 (1) & FSM 22
arcs & hard reset while an AXI/APB transaction is mid-flight. CLAUDE.md
already records that a mid-burst CPU reset "can wedge a crossbar slave
engine"; this is exactly the untested path & \textbf{Test.} One new test
per suite (\code{test_crossbar_errors.py},
\code{test_axil_interconnect.py}, \code{test_axil_to_apb.py},
\code{test_axi4_to_axilite.py}, \code{test_boot_rom.py},
\code{test_sram_controller.py}): start a write burst, drop \code{rst_n}
for 2 cycles while the FSM is in each non-idle state (hold AW
accepted/\allowbreak{}W pending; AR accepted/\allowbreak{}R pending),
release, assert no stuck \code{*valid}/\code{*ready} and that the next
transaction completes. Reset \emph{then} check slave-side state, not
just the master view \\
H2 & FSM arcs, reset in flight & \code{dma_engine.sv} 473 (7:
\code{S_AR},\code{S_AW},\code{S_B},\code{S_CALC},\code{S_R},\code{S_RDRAIN},\code{S_W}
to IDLE); \code{memory_coalescer.sv} 144 (5); \code{uart_controller.sv}
349/522 (3); \code{i2c_bit_engine.sv} 523 (6); \code{rv32i_dcache.sv}
594 (\code{CS_UNCACHED_WR}); \code{mbist_ctrl.sv} 339
(\code{S_INIT},\code{S_PAUSE}) & FSM 24 arcs & the same reset-while-busy
class for the data movers and serial engines & \textbf{Test.}
\code{test_dma.py}: \code{rst_n} pulse during each DMA state of a
3-descriptor run, then verify descriptor queue empty, \code{STATUS}
idle, no stale AXI \code{*valid}. \code{test_memory_coalescer.py},
\code{test_uart.py} (reset mid-frame TX and RX), \code{test_i2c.py}
(reset mid-byte and mid-STOP), \code{test_dcache.py} (reset during an
uncached write), \code{test_mbist_ctrl.py} (reset in INIT and PAUSE).
Shared helper:
\texttt{reset\_\allowbreak{}when\_\allowbreak{}state(dut,\allowbreak{}\ state)} \\
H3 & Error path (SLVERR/\allowbreak{}DECERR observed at SoC level) &
\code{soc_bus}/\code{soc_top}
\texttt{axil\_\allowbreak{}\{dma,\allowbreak{}gpu\}\_\allowbreak{}\{bresp,\allowbreak{}rresp\}[1:\allowbreak{}0]},
\texttt{axil\_\allowbreak{}s\_\allowbreak{}\{bresp,\allowbreak{}rresp\}},
\code{apb_m_pslverr}, \code{bus_mem_bresp[1:0]};
\code{rv32i_core}/\code{rv32i_cpu_top}/\code{rv32i_cache_arbiter}
\code{axi_bresp[0]} & toggle (control) & no non-OKAY response ever
crosses the DMA, GPU or APB-ring buses in a SoC run, and \code{bresp[0]}
never toggles on the CPU/cache AXI ports, so \textbf{no
DECERR/\allowbreak{}EXOKAY
(2\textquotesingle b11/\allowbreak{}2\textquotesingle b01) \emph{write}
response ever reached the CPU master} (SLVERR 2\textquotesingle b10 did,
and \code{rresp} bit 0 does toggle, so read-side DECERR was seen). Unit
suites cover the decode-error generation (\code{axi4_crossbar} line 98
\%, \code{apb_interconnect} line 100 \%), the \emph{consumers} at SoC
level are untested & \textbf{Test.} \code{test_soc_integration.py}: (a)
CPU load and store to an unmapped address, expect the precise
load/\allowbreak{}store-access-fault trap and \code{mcause}; (b) DMA
descriptor with unmapped destination, expect the DMA error status and
IRQ; (c) CPU access to an unmapped APB slot, expect \code{pslverr} to
reach \code{axil_to_apb} and the CPU fault; (d) GPU AXI master read of
an unmapped address \\
H4 & Error path, default arm & \code{rv32i_dcache.sv} 876-877 (uncached
read with RRESP != OKAY returns 0 and does not hang) & line 2, branch &
uncached read error response never injected & \textbf{Test.}
\src{tb/cocotb/mem/test_dcache.py} (\texttt{sim\ dcache}): inject
\code{rresp=SLVERR} and \code{DECERR} on an uncached (MMIO) read; expect
data 0, no stall, \code{CS_DONE} then IDLE, and the same on the refill
path \\
H5 & Flush path & \code{rv32i_dcache.sv} 621
(\texttt{if\ (dc\_\allowbreak{}flush\_\allowbreak{}i\ \&\&\ state\_\allowbreak{}q\ !=\allowbreak{}\ CS\_\allowbreak{}IDLE)\ flush\_\allowbreak{}pending\_\allowbreak{}q\ <=\allowbreak{}\ 1})
& line, branch & CSR \code{dcache_flush} (0x7C0) issued while a
refill/\allowbreak{}writeback is in progress, so the flush is latched
and replayed & \textbf{Test.} \code{test_dcache.py} and
\src{tb/cocotb/cpu/test_csr_access.py}: start a miss to a dirty line,
pulse \code{dc_flush_i} in \code{CS_REFILL} and in \code{CS_WRITEBACK},
expect the line flushed after the miss completes and
\code{flush_pending_q} cleared (the sibling \code{dc_inval_i}-while-busy
line L622 is covered) \\
H6 & FSM arcs, reset during power sequencing & \code{pmu.sv} 509 (11
arcs to \code{DOM_ON}: \code{DOM_PD_SAVE}, \code{DOM_PD_GATE},
\code{DOM_PU_RST}, \code{DOM_PU_CLK}, \code{DOM_PU_RESTORE} for both
domains, plus \code{DOM_PD_ISO} for domain 1; domain 0 is the CPU) & FSM
11 arcs & system reset in the middle of a power-down or power-up
sequence & \textbf{Test.} \code{test_pmu.py}: request
MODE\_\allowbreak{}CPU\_\allowbreak{}OFF / MODE\_\allowbreak{}IDLE,
assert \code{rst_n_i} in each of the 5 intermediate states, expect both
domains back in \code{DOM_ON} with \code{clk_en}/\code{rst_n} at reset
values, no stuck isolation. Add a cocotb check that outputs (registered
per bead \bead{ktwt}) never glitch on the reset edge \\
H7 & CDC, reset in flight & \code{apb_cdc_bridge.sv} 551 (\code{S_DROP}
to IDLE under source-domain reset), cond 541
(\texttt{ack\_\allowbreak{}s\ ||\ !req\_\allowbreak{}fwd\_\allowbreak{}q\ ||\ !m\_\allowbreak{}rst\_\allowbreak{}n\_\allowbreak{}s},
row \texttt{0\ 1\ 0}), 566/585
(\texttt{IDLE\ \&\&\ psel\ \&\&\ !penable}, row \texttt{0\ 1\ 1}), 724
(\texttt{!req\_\allowbreak{}fwd\_\allowbreak{}q\ ||\ !m\_\allowbreak{}rst\_\allowbreak{}n\_\allowbreak{}s},
row \texttt{1\ 0}) & FSM 1 arc, cond 4 rows & the bridge already has
asymmetric-reset directed tests (\code{test_reset_asym_s_held_m_free},
\code{..._staggered_s_then_m}, \code{..._staggered_m_then_s}, \ghref{95}),
so the residue is narrow: source reset landing while the FSM is in
\code{S_DROP}, and \code{req_fwd_q} dropping with neither an ack nor
\code{m_rst_n_s} low (a destination reset shorter than the synchroniser
latency) & \textbf{Test.} \code{test_apb_cdc_bridge.py} with
\texttt{s\_\allowbreak{}clk\ !=\allowbreak{}\ m\_\allowbreak{}clk}: (a)
abort a transfer by resetting \code{m_rst_n}, and assert \code{s_rst}
one cycle later while the source FSM is in \code{S_DROP}; (b) pulse
\code{m_rst_n} for a single destination cycle (shorter than the 2-FF
sync) in the middle of a write and expect either the real response or
\code{pslverr=1}, never a hang \\
H8 & Error path, illegal instruction & \code{rv32i_decode.sv} 351
(SRLI/\allowbreak{}SRAI with funct7 other than 0/0x20), 432 (AND with
funct7 != 0), \code{B159} OP\_IMM / OP\_REG / OP\_SYSTEM \code{default}
arms & line 2, branch 3 & illegal encodings of shift-immediate and AND,
illegal-opcode defaults & \textbf{Test.}
\src{tb/cocotb/cpu/test_decode_corners.py}: add \code{SRLI/SRAI} with
funct7 = 0x01 and 0x40; \code{AND} with funct7 = 0x20; opcode 0x73 with
reserved funct3 = 100. Expect \code{illegal_instruction} trap,
\code{mepc}/\code{mtval}. The \code{default:} arms themselves carry
\texttt{/\allowbreak{}*\ verilator\ coverage\_\allowbreak{}off\ */\allowbreak{}}
(fully enumerated), see W6 \\
H9 & DMA & \code{dma_engine.sv} \code{q_mem[2]}, \code{q_mem[3]}
(src/\allowbreak{}dst/\allowbreak{}len) never toggled; cond 533
(\texttt{!q\_\allowbreak{}empty\ \&\&\ !halted\_\allowbreak{}q}, row
\texttt{1\ 0}); L394 (dead, see W7) & toggle (control) + cond & the
4-entry descriptor queue never holds more than 2 descriptors, so
\textbf{queue full / wrap / back-pressure is untested}; queue non-empty
while halted never seen & \textbf{Test.} \code{test_dma.py}: enqueue 4
descriptors back to back (and a 5th: check the
full/\allowbreak{}overflow behaviour against the spec), halt in the
middle, resume, check ordering and that all four complete; vary
\code{len_words} so \code{q_mem[3].len_words[31:0]} toggles \\
H10 & FSM arcs, debug & \code{rv32i_cpu_top.sv}
677/\allowbreak{}682/\allowbreak{}684/\allowbreak{}691
(\code{halt_cause_lat}: 16 arcs among causes 0x1, 0x2, 0x3, 0x4, 0x8) &
FSM 16 arcs & halt cause re-latched from a \emph{different} cause:
breakpoint 1 after breakpoint 0, EBREAK after step, step after
breakpoint, plain halt after a latched cause & \textbf{Test.}
\src{tb/cocotb/cpu/test_debug_if.py}: matrix of (previous halt cause) x
(next halt reason) over BP0, BP1, step, EBREAK, halt request, reading
\code{DBG_STATUS} after each; this register is the debugger-visible
cause \\
H11 & Dead code & \code{rv32i_pipeline_ex1b.sv} 77-83 (block under
\texttt{if\ (!valid\ ||\ flush\_\allowbreak{}ex\_\allowbreak{}mem)}
whose inner condition needs
\texttt{valid\ \&\&\ !flush\_\allowbreak{}ex\_\allowbreak{}mem}) & line
7, cond 46 \% & \textbf{unreachable by construction}: the inner
\code{if} requires the opposite of the outer one. This is why
\code{ex1b} is the lowest-scoring CPU module (cond 46.2, branch 75.0,
line 82.5) & \textbf{RTL cleanup} (hand to rtl-design-orchestrator) is
the right fix; until then \textbf{waiver} (category b, dead code) with
that reasoning. A test cannot reach it \\
H12 & Coverage of address space & \code{sram_controller.sv} 441-444, 465
(per-group write path), toggle \code{g_stage2_group[n].grp_sel_k} never
toggles for many groups (for example n = 11, 15, 30 and 100 to 126) &
line 4 (generate instances), toggle & the upper SRAM groups were never
written: the write-pipeline group select is only exercised for low
addresses & \textbf{Test.} \code{test_sram_controller.py} (both
\code{SRAM_TARGET} points): address sweep writing one word in every 1/N
group including the last, read back; plus a write to the highest word
and a byte-strobe write there \\
H13 & GPU & \code{warp_scheduler.sv} toggle 23.2 \%:
\code{div_stack[1][*]}, \code{[0][1..3]} never toggle; \code{gpu_top.sv}
\code{if_ar_stuck} never asserts (cond 295 row \texttt{1\ 0}) & toggle
(control), cond & divergence only ever on warp 0 and only the first
stack slot; instruction-fetch stall detector never fires &
\textbf{Test.} \src{tb/cocotb/gpu/test_divergence.py}: run the
divergence kernel on warps 1..7 and with a taken/\allowbreak{}not-taken
pair per warp; \code{test_gpu_top.py}: starve the instruction
\code{ARREADY} for \textgreater{} threshold cycles and expect
\code{if_ar_stuck} and the recovery path. The architecture is one-level
divergence, so slots 1..3 of warp 0 are a \textbf{waiver} (W7b), but
warps 1..7 are not \\
H14 & FSM arcs, I2C slave & \code{i2c_controller.sv} 590
(\code{SLV_ADDR} to IDLE reset), 608 (\code{SLV_RD} to \code{SLV_ADDR},
\code{SLV_WR} to \code{SLV_ADDR}: repeated START inside a slave
transfer), cond 619/\allowbreak{}643/\allowbreak{}647 & FSM 3 arcs, cond
7 rows & loopback-slave handling of a repeated START mid-byte and
mid-ACK; this is not a bin of the I2C functional model either
(\code{loopback_op} is only write/\allowbreak{}read) & \textbf{Test.}
\code{test_i2c.py} in CTRL.LOOPBACK: START, address+R, read one byte
with master ACK, then repeated START to a write address; and the mirror
(write, then repeated START to a read). Add \code{loopback_rstart} to
\code{i2c_cov.py} so the functional model sees it \\
H15 & PLL enable & \code{pll_apb_regs.sv}/\code{pll_subsystem.sv}
\code{pll_enable} never toggles; cond 148
(\texttt{rst\_\allowbreak{}n\_\allowbreak{}i\ \&\ pll\_\allowbreak{}enable},
row \texttt{1\ 0}) & toggle, cond & PLL enable bit is never changed
during any SoC run & \textbf{Test.}
\code{test_pll_regs.py}/\code{test_pll_lock.py}: write the enable bit 0
then 1 with reset released, expect the lock indication to drop and rise
(the area of the \ghref{89} \code{pll_enable} self-brick fix) \\
\end{longtable}
}

\subsection{Toggle coverage: how it was treated}\label{tm:flow:commercial-sim-coverage:4-toggle-coverage-how-it-was-treated}\origsec{4}

Toggle is the lowest metric (module-definition total 77.0 \%, instance
view 55.0 \%) and the one most dominated by things that cannot or should
not be chased:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Wide data and address buses.} VCS reports a bus as untoggled
  by bit range (\code{prdata[31:10]}, \code{awaddr[28:15]},
  \code{q_mem[2].src[31:0]}); a random-data test cannot reasonably
  toggle every bit of a 32-bit address, and the SoC memory map leaves
  the top address bits constant. These rows are \textbf{counted in the
  scores but not listed as holes}.
\item
  \textbf{Tied-off and constant ports.} \code{pready} (tied 1) and
  \code{pslverr} (tied 0) never toggle on any APB peripheral (30 rows);
  constant AXI sideband (burst, size, len, id, prot, wstrb) 277 rows;
  \code{_unused*} signals. Waiver proposals W3/W4 below. They are tool
  artefacts of zero-wait-state, INCR-only, full-strobe design choices,
  not test gaps.
\item
  \textbf{DFT-only ports.} \code{mbist_status_i}, scan and test-mode
  ports are exercised only in the DFT suites; where a bit exists above
  the instantiated slot count it is waivable (W8). Where it is a real
  status field that can be set, it is a hole (section 3.2, MBIST / DFT
  observability).
\item
  \textbf{What \emph{was} chased.} Toggle rows on \textbf{narrow control
  signals} (response codes, \code{*_stuck}, \code{pll_enable},
  state-encoding bits, queue slots, \code{grp_sel_k}, warp-indexed stack
  slots). Those are H3, H9, H12, H13, H15 above. By classification of
  the 1 860 untoggled rows in the 84 \src{rtl/} modules: 386 are
  wide-bus (\textgreater= 8 bits) ranges, 277 AXI
  sideband/\allowbreak{}unused, 30 tied \code{pready}/\code{pslverr},
  and 1 167 are narrower ranges, of which the large majority are address
  low/high bits, debug-CSR mirror bits (\code{dbg_csr_*},
  \code{mstatus}/\code{mcause} bits the tests never set) and
  \code{pc[1:0]} (always 0 on a 4-byte-aligned ISA). I did not walk all
  1 167; the ones named in section 3 are the control-flow relevant ones.
\item
  The Verilator flow used
  \texttt{-\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}coverage-\allowbreak{}max-\allowbreak{}width\ 256}
  to keep wide arrays out. VCS has no equivalent switch; arrays and
  memories are not toggle-instrumented by default
  (\texttt{-\allowbreak{}cm\_\allowbreak{}tgl\ mda} is off), so
  multi-dimensional memories such as \code{sram_controller.mem} do not
  inflate the denominator.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Proposed waivers (nothing is written; for a human to
accept)}\label{tm:flow:commercial-sim-coverage:5-proposed-waivers-nothing-is-written-for-a-human-to-accept}\origsec{5}

Format follows \src{tools/verif/coverage_waivers.txt} (module, kind,
anchor, category b, justification). VCS does not read that file; the VCS
equivalent is an \texttt{urg\ -\allowbreak{}elfile} exclusion file or
\code{-cm_hier} begin/end blocks, to be generated from the accepted list
so the two flows cannot drift.

\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright

\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright

\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}

\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot

encodings in simulation (reset value plus enumerated transitions);
reachable only through state corruption. 33 of the 38 \code{default:}

\code{rv32i_cache_arbiter} 138, \code{rv32i_dcache} 971,

W3 & \code{pready}, \code{pslverr} of APB peripherals & zero-wait-state
peripherals, bus error generated only by the interconnect (H3 covers
that) \\
W4 & constant AXI sideband bits and \code{_unused*} signals & INCR-only,
single-size, full-strobe masters by design \\
W5 & \code{pmu} GPU-domain handshake arms, invalid-state output decode,

(CLAUDE.md item 11, bead \bead{3ogd}: "The GPU needs none"); an invalid

{[}0{]}{[}1..3{]} & provably unreachable (reasoning in section 3).
\textbf{Prefer deleting the dead RTL} over waiving it \\
W8 & DFT-only observability above the instantiated slot count
(\code{mbist_status_i} high bits), scan-mode ports in

}

None of these should be applied to raise a number: the bar for each is
"unreachable by construction", and H3-H10 are not waivable (a test can
reach them).

\subsection{Caveats and tool behaviour that affects reading the
numbers}\label{tm:flow:commercial-sim-coverage:6-caveats-and-tool-behaviour-that-affects-reading-the-numbers}\origsec{6}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{C1. Shapes.} 17 modules have more than one parameter shape
  (for example \code{sram_controller} 3, \code{mbist_ctrl} 5,
  \code{apb_interconnect} 5, \code{crypto_accel} 5, \code{mem_collar} 8,
  \code{cdc_gray_fifo} 8). The module score is urg\textquotesingle s
  merged top row; the hole counts are "uncovered in every shape that
  lists the point", so a point hit in only one shape is not listed.
  \code{vcs_cov_holes.py} also records the shape-only holes
  (\code{*_shape_only} keys in its JSON); they are mostly rarely-used
  configurations (for example \code{crypto_accel} with
  \code{EN_AES}/\code{EN_SHA} off).
\item
  \textbf{C2. Generate loops count per instance.} A line inside a
  \texttt{for\ .\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}\ generate} is
  listed once per iteration and counted uncovered if any iteration
  missed it. This is why \code{sram_controller} (128 groups) and
  \code{pmu} (two domains) show line holes that Verilator, which folds
  identical points, did not.
\item
  \textbf{C3. Crypto condition rows.} \code{crypto_accel} L511
  (\code{op_legal_w}) lists row \texttt{1\ 0} ("AES-legal and not
  SHA-legal") as never covered. That contradicts \code{aes128_core} line
  100 \% and 51 passing crypto tests, so it is treated as a
  merge/\allowbreak{}shape bookkeeping artefact of the
  \code{EN_AES}/\code{EN_SHA} parameters and \textbf{not} reported as a
  hole. Needs a per-shape look in Verdi before anything is concluded
  from it.
\item
  \textbf{C4. Missing metric objects.} \code{axi4_crossbar} has no COND
  or BRANCH objects in the vdb (urg: "coverage shape is not there").
  Measured with and without \code{-cm_hier}, so it is not the scope
  file, and a minimal \code{always_comb} \code{if/else} does produce
  both metrics, so it is not a blanket \code{always_comb} limitation.
  The cause was not established (the crossbar uses
  \texttt{for\ (int\ .\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{})} loops
  inside \code{always_comb} and a function call in the condition). Its
  branch and condition coverage is therefore \textbf{not measured} by
  this run; only line, toggle and FSM are.
\item
  \textbf{C5. Verilator pragmas and waivers are not honoured by VCS}
  (see 2.1), so VCS lists defaults that the Verilator report already
  hides.
\item
  \textbf{C6. \texttt{-\allowbreak{}flex\_\allowbreak{}merge\ modules}
  is LCA} and the merged \code{.vdb} is written next to the report
  directory (\code{report/<area>.vdb}). Instance-level hierarchies of
  different testbenches are reported side by side, not merged.
\item
  \textbf{C7. Tests that share a build.} Per-test attribution is
  available (\code{tests.txt}, one row per \code{-cm_name}), but this
  review ranks by module, not by which test hits what.
\item
  \textbf{C8. Not run here:} Xcelium/\allowbreak{}imc coverage (user
  decision); the cocotb functional-coverage YAML export
  (\code{FUNC_COV_DIR} was not set, so no functional percentage is
  quoted for this run); formal; gate-level.
\end{itemize}

\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Gate
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Relation to the code-coverage holes
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\src{tb/cocotb/soc/func_cov.py} & plumbing over \code{cocotb-coverage}
1.2.0 (coverpoints, crosses, YAML export through \code{FUNC_COV_DIR});
never a hard dependency of a simulation & n/a & opt-in, so an ordinary
run (including this one) exports nothing \\
\src{tb/cocotb/soc/i2c_cov.py} & \textbf{I2C only}: 138 bins sampled
from the BFM event stream, APB transfers and assertion-backed scenario
samples (\src{docs/verification/I2C_FUNCTIONAL_COVERAGE_MODEL.md} (\cref{tm:flow:i2c-functional-coverage-model})) &
\src{tools/verif/functional_coverage_gates.txt}: \code{i2c}, 100 \%,
\code{min_bins} 138 & \code{i2c_controller} is 100 \% line, 95.3 \%
cond, 95.7 \% branch but \textbf{62.5 \% FSM}: the missing arcs (H14)
are the loopback slave\textquotesingle s repeated START, which the model
does not have a bin for (\code{loopback_op} is only
write/\allowbreak{}read). A 100 \% functional gate on a thin model did
not catch it \\
Everything else (UART, SPI, DMA, PMU, crypto, NPU, caches, GPU, fabric)
& no covergroups & none & the holes H1-H10 are all in blocks with
\textbf{no} functional model; code coverage is the only coverage
evidence for them. \src{docs/verification/FUNCTIONAL_COVERAGE.md} (\cref{tm:flow:functional-coverage})
section 4 is the rollout recipe; the highest-value next models are the
AXI/APB fabric (response codes x channel x reset-phase, which would have
flagged H1 and H3), the DMA (queue depth x burst length) and the PMU
(sequence state x reset phase) \\
\end{longtable}
}

}

Code coverage says which code ran; it cannot say that NPU KLEN = 1 and
KLEN = 63 both ran, that all four AES key sizes or all SHA block counts
ran, or that every response code was seen on every channel. Those need
covergroups, which this pass deliberately does not write.

\subsection{Reproducibility and provenance}\label{tm:flow:commercial-sim-coverage:8-reproducibility-and-provenance}\origsec{8}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Instrumented regression and merge: \src{tools/eda/vcs_coverage.py}
  (new), job lists from \src{tools/eda/regress_specs.py}, driver
  \src{tools/eda/run_cocotb_targets.py}, flags in
  \src{tb/cocotb/common/sim_portability.mk}.
\item
  Hole digest: \src{tools/eda/vcs_cov_holes.py} (new) reads only
  urg\textquotesingle s text output.
\item
  Databases and reports are not committed (\src{.gitignore}:
  \code{sim_build*}, \code{*.vdb/}, \code{urgReport*/},
  \code{simv.daidir/}, \code{vcs_cov_scope_*}). They are regenerated by
  the first command in section 1 in about 24 minutes.
\item
  Artifact provenance: the coverage scope lists and \code{-cm_hier}
  files are regenerated by \code{make} at each build from the checked-in
  \src{rtl/} tree; no pre-generated artifact is consumed.
\end{itemize}
\end{relocated}

\begin{relocated}{docs/verification/FUNCTIONAL_COVERAGE.md}
% Converted from docs/verification/FUNCTIONAL_COVERAGE.md by tools/docs/md2tex.py; edit this file, not the original.
\section{Functional coverage in the cocotb flow (bead h367)}\label{tm:flow:functional-coverage}

\src{tools/verif/coverage_report.py} measures Verilator \textbf{code}
coverage (line, toggle). Until this change nothing measured
\textbf{functional} coverage: no covergroup, so the verification
sign-off criterion
\texttt{functional\_\allowbreak{}coverage\_\allowbreak{}pct:\allowbreak{}\ 100}
could not be met for any peripheral. This document records the mechanism
evaluation, the chosen design and how another peripheral adds a model.
The pilot is I2C (\code{I2C_FUNCTIONAL_COVERAGE_MODEL.md} (\cref{tm:flow:i2c-functional-coverage-model})).

\subsection{Evaluation}\label{tm:flow:functional-coverage:1-evaluation}\origsec{1}

{\footnotesize\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}

{
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*348/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*130/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*502/1000\relax}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Option
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Verdict
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Evidence
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\textbf{\code{cocotb-coverage} 1.2.0} (PyPI) & \textbf{Chosen} &
\texttt{Requires-\allowbreak{}Dist:\allowbreak{}\ cocotb\ \textasciitilde{}=\allowbreak{}1.\allowbreak{}8}
(so \code{>=1.8,<2.0}), matching \src{requirements.txt}
(\code{cocotb>=1.8.0,<2.0}) and the installed cocotb 1.9.2. Pure Python;
\code{cocotb_coverage.coverage} does not even import cocotb. CoverPoint
/ CoverCross / \code{at_least} / weights, YAML and XML export, and a
\code{merge_coverage} for runs. Verified here: installs, imports,
samples inside a Verilator cocotb 1.9.2 simulation, exports, and its
merge agrees with this repo\textquotesingle s merge (a unit test). \\
\code{cocotb-coverage} 2.0 (2025-10-03) & Not usable now & Requires
\code{cocotb>=2} and Python \code{>=3.11}. This repo is pinned to cocotb
1.x (cocotb 2 removed the \code{units=} kwarg used throughout \src{tb/})
and CI runs Python 3.10. Revisit when the cocotb 2 migration happens;
the YAML schema is unchanged, so only \code{func_cov.py} would be
touched. \\
pyuvm (4.0.1 installed, \code{<3.1} pinned) & No & No covergroup
facility: the package has the UVM component, phase, TLM, factory and
register-model classes only (a search for covergroup / coverpoint over
the installed package finds nothing). \\
SystemVerilog covergroups under Verilator & No & Verilator 5.048 (the
flake pin) parses and \textbf{ignores} them:
\texttt{\%Warning-\allowbreak{}COVERIGN:\allowbreak{}\ Ignoring\ unsupported:\allowbreak{}\ coverpoint}
/ \texttt{cover\ bin\ specification} /
\texttt{coverage\ clocking\ event} (reproduced with a 2-bin covergroup).
They would also have to live in RTL or an SV testbench, which this pilot
must not touch. \\
In-repo collector & No & Would re-implement bins, crosses,
\code{at_least}, merge and export that the library already has and
tests. The one design hedge kept: the merge/\allowbreak{}gate script
reads the plain exported YAML schema and does \textbf{not} import the
library, so the sampling layer can be replaced without touching the
gate. \\
\end{longtable}
}

}

\textbf{Recommendation: \code{cocotb-coverage==1.2.0}}, pinned exactly
in \src{requirements.txt} (1.x is frozen at 1.2.0, 2023-11-15; 2.0 is
the maintained line but needs cocotb 2).

Known library traits that shaped the glue
(\src{tb/cocotb/soc/func_cov.py}):

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \code{CoverageDB} is a process-wide singleton and cocotb runs every
  test of a MODULE in one simulator process, so the database is
  cumulative across a suite; export once, at process exit.
\item
  Sampling is by decorated function. \code{CoverPoint} runs
  \code{inspect.getmembers} on the first argument the first time it is
  called, so samplers take plain Python values (never a DUT handle).
\item
  The first matching bin wins (\code{inj}), which makes ordered range
  bins exclusive.
\item
  A cross reads the \code{new_hits} of its coverpoints, so the
  coverpoints must be sampled first (\code{func_cov.pair} orders the
  decorators correctly).
\item
  Cross bins are exported as \code{str(tuple)} keys, for example
  \texttt{('write',\allowbreak{}\ 'ack')}.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Flow}\label{tm:flow:functional-coverage:2-flow}\origsec{2}

\begin{lstlisting}[language={}]
 cocotb test --> BFM observers --> func_cov samplers --> coverage_db (per process)
                                                              | atexit, if FUNC_COV_DIR is set
                                                              v
                                       $FUNC_COV_DIR/<module>__<pid>.yml   (one per simulator process)
                                                              | CI: uploaded by each shard
                                                              v
        tools/verif/functional_coverage_report.py  --gate  -->  report .md/.json + merged .yml
\end{lstlisting}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Sampling is on by default and costs a few dict updates per event.
  \textbf{Export is opt-in} through \code{FUNC_COV_DIR}; unset means
  nothing is written, so a local run leaves no files.
\item
  If \code{cocotb_coverage} is not installed every sampler is a no-op
  and the suites still run. The CI gate is what fails (a required group
  with no data is exit 2), so a missing package cannot turn it green.
\item
  Local run:
  \texttt{FUNC\_\allowbreak{}COV\_\allowbreak{}DIR=\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}tmp/\allowbreak{}fc\ nix\ develop\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}command\ make\ -\allowbreak{}C\ tb/\allowbreak{}cocotb/\allowbreak{}soc\ i2c},
  then
  \texttt{python3\ tools/\allowbreak{}verif/\allowbreak{}functional\_\allowbreak{}coverage\_\allowbreak{}report.\allowbreak{}py\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}in\ /\allowbreak{}tmp/\allowbreak{}fc\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}waivers\ tools/\allowbreak{}verif/\allowbreak{}functional\_\allowbreak{}coverage\_\allowbreak{}waivers.\allowbreak{}txt\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}gates\ tools/\allowbreak{}verif/\allowbreak{}functional\_\allowbreak{}coverage\_\allowbreak{}gates.\allowbreak{}txt\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}gate}.
  \code{PYTHONPATH} must reach a Python that has \code{cocotb-coverage}
  (it is in \src{requirements.txt}).
\item
  CI: \code{cocotb.yml} (per pull request). Each \code{soc_shard} leg
  sets \code{FUNC_COV_DIR}, installs the package and uploads
  \code{func_cov/}; the \code{soc_all} aggregate job runs the gate over
  all shards and publishes the report as the \code{functional-coverage}
  artifact (and in the job summary). The instrumented
  \code{soc_coverage.yml} is not used: sampling needs no instrumented
  build, and cocotb.yml already runs per pull request.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{The gate}\label{tm:flow:functional-coverage:3-the-gate}\origsec{3}

\texttt{functional\_\allowbreak{}coverage\_\allowbreak{}report.\allowbreak{}py\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}gate}
merges every YAML (hits add; totals are recomputed from the hits, never
taken from the inputs; a coverpoint declared differently in two files is
exit 2), then:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \src{tools/verif/functional_coverage_gates.txt}
  (\texttt{group\ |\ floor\_\allowbreak{}pct\ |\ min\_\allowbreak{}bins\ |\ why}):
  every listed group is \textbf{required} (no data is exit 2), its
  adjusted percentage must reach \code{floor_pct}, and its model must
  keep at least \code{min_bins} bins, so deleting coverpoints cannot buy
  a pass.
\item
  \src{tools/verif/functional_coverage_waivers.txt}
  (\texttt{item\ |\ bin\ |\ b\ |\ why}): category \code{b} only,
  unreachable by design, with a reason. A bin that is merely not hit
  needs a test. A waiver for a bin that does not exist, or that was hit,
  is stale and fails the gate.
\end{itemize}

Exit status: 0 pass, 1 gate red, 2 could not evaluate (same convention
as \code{coverage_report.py}).

\subsection{Rolling out to another peripheral}\label{tm:flow:functional-coverage:4-rolling-out-to-another-peripheral}\origsec{4}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Model first.} Write
  \code{docs/verification/<PERIPH>_FUNCTIONAL_COVERAGE_MODEL.md}: one
  row per coverpoint with its bins and why each matters (spec section or
  RTL header). Derive bins from the register map and the behaviours the
  spec promises, not from what the tests happen to do.
\item
  \textbf{\code{tb/cocotb/soc/<periph>_cov.py}}, modelled on
  \code{i2c_cov.py}: declare the coverpoints at module level with
  \code{func_cov.point} / \code{ranges} / \code{pair} (names
  \code{<periph>.<group>.<coverpoint>}), put the samplers in a small
  observer class, create
  \texttt{COV\ =\allowbreak{}\ .\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}.\allowbreak{}}
  and call
  \texttt{func\_\allowbreak{}cov.\allowbreak{}enable\_\allowbreak{}export(os.\allowbreak{}environ.\allowbreak{}get("MODULE",\allowbreak{}\ "<periph>"))}.
\item
  \textbf{Sampling points, in order of preference:} a. observers on the
  existing BFMs. \code{APB4Master.add_observer(cb)} gives
  \texttt{cb(is\_\allowbreak{}write,\allowbreak{}\ addr,\allowbreak{}\ data,\allowbreak{}\ strb,\allowbreak{}\ ok)}
  after every transfer; a pin-level BFM can offer the same
  (\code{I2CSlave.add_observer}). Sample only fields the byte strobes
  cover. b. the tests\textquotesingle{} side-effect-free register peek,
  via
  \texttt{COV.\allowbreak{}reg\_\allowbreak{}read(addr,\allowbreak{}\ value)}.
  c. an explicit scenario call
  (\texttt{COV.\allowbreak{}arb\_\allowbreak{}loss("data\_\allowbreak{}bit")})
  \textbf{only} for a fact the pins cannot show, placed \textbf{after}
  the test\textquotesingle s own assertions for that scenario, so a
  failing scenario samples nothing.
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{COV.\allowbreak{}attach(apb,\allowbreak{}\ bfm)}}
  where the test builds its BFMs (per test: a new BFM per test, the
  database is per process).
\item
  \textbf{Run it, read the holes.} Each uncovered bin is a test to write
  or, if it cannot happen, a category-\code{b} waiver with the reason.
  Mutation-check any new test as the other suites are.
\item
  Add the group to \code{functional_coverage_gates.txt} with the
  measured \code{min_bins} (and a floor of 100 after waivers), and the
  waivers to \code{functional_coverage_waivers.txt}. If you add a cocotb
  suite, add it to \code{SOC_CI_SUITES} in \src{tb/cocotb/soc/Makefile}
  and to \src{tools/verif/soc_shard_weights.json} and
  \code{soc_shard_weights_plain.json}.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{What this does not do}\label{tm:flow:functional-coverage:5-what-this-does-not-do}\origsec{5}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Coverage is only as good as the model: 100 \% of a thin model is not
  verification closure. Bins come from the spec; the model document says
  what each one is for.
\item
  Scenario samples (3c) are declarations backed by the
  test\textquotesingle s assertions, not observations.
\item
  No cross-run history or trend; the report is per run (the merged YAML
  is the artifact).
\item
  Only I2C is modelled. Other peripherals have no covergroups until they
  follow section 4.
\end{itemize}
\end{relocated}

\begin{relocated}{docs/verification/I2C_FUNCTIONAL_COVERAGE_MODEL.md}
% Converted from docs/verification/I2C_FUNCTIONAL_COVERAGE_MODEL.md by tools/docs/md2tex.py; edit this file, not the original.
\section{I2C functional-coverage model (bead h367 pilot)}\label{tm:flow:i2c-functional-coverage-model}

DUT: \src{rtl/periph/i2c_controller.sv} (+ \code{i2c_bit_engine.sv}),
I2C master, APB4 slave, 12 registers. Implementation:
\src{tb/cocotb/soc/i2c_cov.py}. Mechanism, flow and rollout:
\code{FUNCTIONAL_COVERAGE.md} (\cref{tm:flow:functional-coverage}). The bins come from the controller header
(the spec of record for this IP) and the I2C protocol, not from what the
tests happen to do. Names are \code{i2c.<group>.<coverpoint>}.

Sources of samples: \textbf{bus} = the \code{I2CSlave} BFM event stream;
\textbf{apb} = \code{APB4Master} transfers and the
tests\textquotesingle{} \code{_peek}; \textbf{scenario} = a call placed
after the test\textquotesingle s own assertions. No RTL is touched and
no DUT signal is read by the model.

Result of the first complete run: \textbf{138 bins, all hit, no waivers}
(table at the end).

\subsection{bus: what the master did on the wire (source: bus)}\label{tm:flow:i2c-functional-coverage-model:bus-what-the-master-did-on-the-wire-source-bus}

{\footnotesize\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}

{
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*272/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*342/1000\relax}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-4\tabcolsep)*366/1000\relax}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Coverpoint
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Bins
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Why
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\code{start_kind} & \code{start}, \code{rstart_after_write},
\code{rstart_after_read} & START and the two repeated-START contexts. A
START that follows an aborted transfer is not counted as a repeated
START. \\
\code{addr_dir}, \code{addr_ack}, cross \code{addr_result} &
write/\allowbreak{}read x ack/nack (4 cross bins) & Address phase
outcome per direction. \\
\code{wr_data_ack} & ack, nack & A slave NACK on write data. \\
\code{rd_master_ack} & ack, nack & The master ACKs every read byte
except a NACK\_LAST final one. \\
\code{wr_bytes} & 0 (address only), 1, 2, 3-4, 5-8, 9-16, 17+ & Per
write segment with an ACKed address; 8 is the FIFO depth, 9-16 and 17+
need FIFO refill during the transfer. Counts every data byte on the
wire. \\
\code{rd_bytes} & 1, 2, 3-4, 5-8, 9-16, 17+ & Same for reads (RX FIFO
full / drain). \\
\code{segments_per_txn} & 1, 2, 3+ & START..STOP: plain, one repeated
START, a chain. \\
\code{stop_after} & write, read, addr\_nack & What the STOP closed. \\
\code{stretch_at} & addr\_bit, addr\_ack, wr\_bit, wr\_ack, rd\_bit,
rd\_ack & Mandatory clock stretching, where the slave held SCL.
\code{STRETCH} is reported by the BFM (not in \code{events}). \\
\code{stretch_mode} & single\_\allowbreak{}fall,
every\_\allowbreak{}fall & One stretch vs a slave that stretches every
bit. \\
\end{longtable}
}

}

\code{nack_where} & address, write\_\allowbreak{}data & bus & Where the
slave NACKed. \\
\code{nack_state} & bus\_held, stop\_sent & apb & After a NACK the
master either holds the bus (no STOP requested) or released it. \\
\code{arb_loss} & address\_\allowbreak{}bit, data\_bit,
read\_\allowbreak{}nack\_\allowbreak{}slot, rstart\_\allowbreak{}setup &
scenario & Where arbitration was lost; sampled after
\code{arb_lost}-only and released-lines assertions. \\
\code{timeout_cause} & scl\_\allowbreak{}stretched,
bus\_\allowbreak{}busy\_\allowbreak{}wait, tx\_\allowbreak{}starve,
rx\_full & scenario & Which bounded wait expired; sampled after the
sticky \code{timeout} assertion. \\
\code{cmd_ignored} & disabled, illegal\_\allowbreak{}shape,
no\_\allowbreak{}strobe, busy & scenario & A CMD write silently dropped;
sampled after the bus proved quiet. \\
\code{abort} & en\_\allowbreak{}cleared, reset & scenario & A transfer
aborted by CTRL.EN = 0 or by reset, after the post-abort state
checks. \\
\end{longtable}
}

}

\subsection{Known limits of the model}\label{tm:flow:i2c-functional-coverage-model:known-limits-of-the-model}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  It checks that scenarios occurred, not that the DUT\textquotesingle s
  data was right: that stays the tests\textquotesingle{} job.
\item
  \code{arb_loss}, \code{timeout_cause}, \code{cmd_ignored},
  \code{abort} and \code{soc.path} are declarations made after the
  test\textquotesingle s assertions, not independent observations.
\item
  Not modelled: back-to-back START timing (tBUF/\allowbreak{}tHD;STA,
  measured by dedicated tests), 10-bit addressing (not implemented),
  multi-master beyond arbitration loss, SDA setup/\allowbreak{}hold.
\end{itemize}
\end{relocated}

\begin{relocated}{docs/verification/SOC_COVERAGE_REPORT.md}
% Converted from docs/verification/SOC_COVERAGE_REPORT.md by tools/docs/md2tex.py; edit this file, not the original.
\section{SoC line + toggle coverage report (GH \#216)}\label{tm:flow:soc-coverage-report}

Bead \code{nkj7}. Measured 2026-10-06 on branch
\code{feat/soc-coverage-216}.
\texttt{Doc:\allowbreak{}\ ds:\allowbreak{}ch:\allowbreak{}verification-\allowbreak{}plan\ (line\ 362\ of\ the\ original\ file)}
sets a

\begin{quote}
95 \% RTL line-coverage exit criterion; until now only the 9 CPU cocotb
modules (\texttt{sim/\allowbreak{}Makefile\ coverage}) were
instrumented. This report covers the whole \src{tb/cocotb/soc}
regression (\code{soc_all_ci}).
\end{quote}

\subsection{Method}\label{tm:flow:soc-coverage-report:method}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \code{COVERAGE=1} in \src{tb/cocotb/soc/Makefile} adds
  \texttt{-\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}coverage-\allowbreak{}line\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}coverage-\allowbreak{}toggle\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}coverage-\allowbreak{}max-\allowbreak{}width\ 256}
  to \code{COMPILE_ARGS} (not \code{EXTRA_ARGS}: most targets override
  that on the command line). Every \code{SIM_BUILD} (since bead
  \bead{b079} a content-keyed \code{sim_build_<TOPLEVEL>_<hash>}, shared
  by suites with an identical model; the \code{.dat} name carries MODULE
  so sharing does not clash) gets a \code{_cov} suffix, so an
  instrumented build can never reuse an uninstrumented \code{Vtop} (the
  \prref{142} stale-\code{Vtop} false-pass class). Each simulation writes
  its own \code{<build>__<module>.dat} through the runtime plusarg
  \code{+verilator+coverage+file+} (honoured by cocotb
  1.9.2\textquotesingle s \code{verilator.cpp}).
  \texttt{-\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}coverage-\allowbreak{}max-\allowbreak{}width\ 256}
  keeps the DMA\textquotesingle s 8192-bit \code{linebuf} and the SRAM
  model arrays out of toggle coverage.
\item
  \texttt{make\ soc\_\allowbreak{}coverage} runs the whole
  \code{soc_all_ci} instrumented, merges all \code{.dat} files with
  \texttt{verilator\_\allowbreak{}coverage\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}write},
  and renders the tables below with
  \src{tools/verif/coverage_report.py}. It is not part of
  \code{soc_all_ci} / \code{soc_all}; CI runs it nightly
  (\src{.github/workflows/soc_coverage.yml}).
\item
  \textbf{CI is sharded (bead \bead{9ayu.1}).} The workflow does not run
  \code{soc_coverage} as one job: it runs \code{NSHARDS} (8)
  \code{soc_shard} legs
  (\texttt{make\ soc\_\allowbreak{}coverage\_\allowbreak{}shard\ SHARD=\allowbreak{}k\ NSHARDS=\allowbreak{}8})
  and the \code{cpu_gpu} job in parallel, then a final job (name
  \texttt{SoC\ line\ +\allowbreak{}\ toggle\ coverage}, the required
  check) merges every shard\textquotesingle s \code{.dat} with the same
  \texttt{verilator\_\allowbreak{}coverage\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}write}
  and runs the unchanged report and gate. The suite list is the single
  variable \code{SOC_CI_SUITES} in \src{tb/cocotb/soc/Makefile}
  (\code{soc_all_ci} runs all of it). The slice for shard k comes from
  \src{tools/verif/soc_shards.py} (deterministic greedy balance on the
  measured timings in \src{tools/verif/soc_shard_weights.json}; a new
  suite needs no edit there, it gets a default weight). The final job
  fails unless the union of the suites the shards ran equals
  \code{SOC_CI_SUITES}
  (\texttt{soc\_\allowbreak{}shards.\allowbreak{}py\ union}) and the
  pass count SUMMED over all shard logs reaches \code{PASS_FLOOR}.
  \texttt{make\ soc\_\allowbreak{}coverage} itself is unchanged for
  local use.
\item
  Line \% = \code{v_line} + \code{v_branch} points; toggle \% =
  \code{v_toggle} points (per bit, per direction, as Verilator counts
  them). A point is the same point in every instance and testbench and
  is hit if any instance hit it; \code{<module>__<params>}
  specialisations fold into the base module.
\item
  Triaged trees: \src{rtl/soc}, \src{rtl/periph}, \src{rtl/npu} (line +
  control-toggle gate). \src{rtl/cpu}, \src{rtl/mem}, \src{rtl/gpu}:
  \textbf{line gate only} since bead \bead{a5ze} slice 2 (see "Gate
  decision"); no toggle gate, no toggle triage. Excluded: \src{tb/},
  \src{sim/}, behavioural SRAM models.
\end{itemize}

Exact run command (15 GB host, \code{SIM_BUILD_ROOT} on the big disk,
never concurrent with another soc sim):

\begin{lstlisting}[language=bash]
nix develop --command make -C tb/cocotb/soc soc_coverage SIM_BUILD_ROOT=/nobackup/claude_sim_build/soc_cov
# outputs: $SIM_BUILD_ROOT/coverage/{merged.dat,coverage_report.md,coverage_report.json}
\end{lstlisting}

Measured run: 53 suites, \textbf{480 passed, 0 failed, 16 skipped} (the
same 480/0/16 as the uninstrumented regression), 53 per-simulation
\code{.dat} files merged, wall time about 85 min (5 095 s) with a cold
build, 5.9 GB of build output. Nix Verilator 5.048 handled
\code{--coverage} without the bead \bead{hg2} crash: the Makefile
already passes
\texttt{PYTHON3=\allowbreak{}\$(SIM\_\allowbreak{}PYTHON3)}, and
\code{verilator_coverage} from the same nix profile merges fine, so no
source-built Verilator is needed.

\textbf{Re-measured 2026-10-07 after bead \bead{8riq}} (same command,
\code{SIM_BUILD_ROOT} from clean, all other RTL unchanged): 54 suites (+
the new \code{axil_apb_fabric}), \textbf{511 passed by the CI log-grep
formula, 0 failed, 17 skipped} (512 \code{PASS} rows in the
\code{TESTS=} blocks: the extra one is the \code{expect_fail} guard
\code{test_ring_unmapped_read_single_beat}, which cocotb reports as a
pass but the CI grep does not count; +31 = 14 fabric + 6
register\_\allowbreak{}bank + 2 axil\_\allowbreak{}to\_\allowbreak{}apb
+ 10 apb\_\allowbreak{}interconnect sweep points, minus that 1,
reconciling exactly against the prior 480). 55 \code{.dat} files merged.
The tables below are this run.

The formerly uncovered points now hit: \code{axi_lite_interconnect} L206
(W\_AW stall), L207 (W\_DATA stall), L326 (R\_AR stall);
\code{axi_lite_register_bank} L156/L158 (\code{w_now} low: W captured
before AW) and L205 (\code{RD_RESP} with \code{rready} low);
\code{axil_to_apb} L175/\allowbreak{}L176/\allowbreak{}L177 (ACCESS wait
state, \code{S_WRESP}/\code{S_RRESP} held by a stalled master).
\code{s_axil_bresp}/\code{rresp} of the ring now leave OKAY end to end
(EXOKAY/\allowbreak{}SLVERR/\allowbreak{}DECERR from a slave; DECERR
from the ring\textquotesingle s own decode; SLVERR from
\code{axil_to_apb} for a slave \code{pslverr} and for
\code{apb_interconnect}\textquotesingle s unclaimed-slot
\code{pslverr_o}). \code{apb_interconnect}
\code{pwdata_o}/\code{pstrb_o}/\code{pwrite_o}/\code{penable_o}/\code{paddr_o}
are now driven and checked on every slave copy. The two
\code{axi_lite_register_bank} response ports are waived (tied to OKAY by
design, one new category-b waiver); the rest of the toggle gain is real
stimulus.

\textbf{Waiver correction (partially addresses \ghref{222}, W1).} The
\code{soc_bus} waiver
\texttt{\textasciicircum{}(apb\_\allowbreak{}pslverr|apb\_\allowbreak{}m\_\allowbreak{}pslverr)[\textbackslash{}[:\allowbreak{}]}
was too wide: \code{apb_m_pslverr} is
\code{apb_interconnect}\textquotesingle s \code{pslverr_o}, which is 1
on an unmapped APB access, so it is not a hard-wired constant. It is
narrowed to
\texttt{\textasciicircum{}apb\_\allowbreak{}pslverr[\textbackslash{}[:\allowbreak{}]}
and \code{apb_m_pslverr} (2 points) now shows as a never-toggling signal
in \code{soc_bus}. It still does not toggle in the full regression, and
the reason is the memory map, not a missing test: the only hole in the
APB window (\code{0x2000_5000-0x2000_5FFF}) is claimed by the DMA slot
of the AXI-Lite ring (last match wins in \code{axi_lite_interconnect}),
so no ring access can ever reach an unclaimed APB offset in the
integrated SoC, and every APB slave ties \code{pslverr} to 0. The same
\code{apb_interconnect} -\textgreater{} \code{axil_to_apb}
-\textgreater{} SLVERR path is covered in \code{axil_apb_fabric}, which
instantiates the real modules with a hole in the APB window. Making
\code{soc_bus.apb_m_pslverr} toggle would need either a map change or an
unmapped-APB stimulus injected below the ring; \textbf{Decided
2026-10-07 (user): waive, do not change the map} -\/-
\code{apb_m_pslverr} is now waived in \code{soc_bus} with this map-based
justification (W1 closed). Validated against \prref{226}\textquotesingle s
CI coverage data: the waiver matches, no waiver is stale, and
\code{apb_m_pslverr} no longer appears as uncovered.

Non-vacuity: the known gaps show up in the raw (pre-waiver) report:
\code{timer_irq} never toggles, \code{i2c_scl_o} / \code{i2c_sda_o}
never toggle, and the DMA early-RLAST branch is not in the uncovered
list (covered since bead \bead{wdmo}, \prref{217}).

Formerly uncovered \code{soc_top} points now hit (all behaviourally
checked, not just toggled):

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \code{soc_top.sv} L1450-L1456 (GPU isolation clamps, 7
  \code{cond_then} arms): PMU GPU power cycle with
  \texttt{gpu\_\allowbreak{}irq\_\allowbreak{}o\ =\allowbreak{}=\allowbreak{}\ gpu\_\allowbreak{}irq\_\allowbreak{}raw\ \&\ !gpu\_\allowbreak{}iso\_\allowbreak{}en}
  asserted on every cycle and the clamp seen acting
  (\texttt{raw=\allowbreak{}1,\allowbreak{}\ out=\allowbreak{}0,\allowbreak{}\ iso=\allowbreak{}1})
  in the iso-before-reset window; sequence order
  \texttt{ret\_\allowbreak{}save\ <\ iso\_\allowbreak{}en\ <\ clk\ gate\ <\ reset}
  down and the reverse up checked from the testbench.
\item
  \code{pmu_gpu_iso_en}, \code{pmu_gpu_ret_save},
  \code{pmu_gpu_ret_restore}, \code{timer_irq} (+\code{_cpu_sync}),
  \code{uart_irq}, \code{spi_irq}, \code{dma_irq}: the CPU takes the
  trap for each (ISR committed, \code{mcause} 0x8000\_\allowbreak{}0007
  for the timer\textquotesingle s direct MTIP and
  0x8000\_\allowbreak{}000B for the three routed through
  \code{interrupt_controller}, pending bit logged, source nets sampled
  at trap entry and after the handler).
\item
  \code{spi_miso_i}: non-loopback SPI against a testbench slave;
  firmware reads back 0xA6, the slave checks MOSI 0x3C and CS framing.
\item
  Debug path
  \texttt{apb\_\allowbreak{}\{psel,\allowbreak{}penable,\allowbreak{}pwrite,\allowbreak{}paddr,\allowbreak{}pwdata\}\_\allowbreak{}i},
  \code{dbg_bridge_m_*}, and \textbf{\code{apb_pslverr_o},
  \code{dbg_bridge_m_pslverr}} (un-waived by \ghref{222} W2): PSLVERR on a
  write to each RO CSR debug register (\code{rv32i_cpu_top.sv:64,330})
  and the \code{apb_cdc_bridge} force-complete PSLVERR with the
  destination (CPU-domain) in reset, both before the request and with
  the reset asserted 1-6 cycles into it.
\item
  \code{pll_m_pwdata}/\code{pll2_m_pwdata}/\code{paddr} and
  \code{pll_subsystem}/\code{pll_apb_regs} divider fields: both
  PLLs\textquotesingle{} CONTROL written through the fabric (read back,
  locked flag re-checked), \code{pll_fb_div}/\code{pll_post_div} of each
  instance checked after every write and the other instance proven
  untouched. \code{pll_clkgen_stub} is a pass-through by design, so the
  "output" observed is the divider ports at \code{pll_clkgen} plus the
  lock flag, not a frequency change.
\end{itemize}

Still open in this bead\textquotesingle s scope: \code{i2c_*_oe_o} (pad
drive, bead \bead{pnfw}), ROM write channel \code{bus_rom_aw*/w*/b*}
(bead \bead{ej6j}, unit level), \code{uart_rx_i} (bead \bead{rqvo}),
\code{pll_m_prdata}/\code{pready} partial points.
\code{interrupt_controller} and \code{pmu} toggle gains are small
because their uncovered points are register-bank
data/\allowbreak{}address bits, not integration paths.

\textbf{Finding (no RTL change made).} The GPU\textquotesingle s flops
are async-reset, so the PMU\textquotesingle s domain reset clears all
GPU state (the level-held irq latch, \code{irq_enable}, \code{done})
even though the clock is gated first. \code{pmu.sv}\textquotesingle s
header and \src{docs/POWER_DOMAIN_EVALUATION.md} (\cref{tm:pd:power-domain-evaluation}) claim state survives an
off/on cycle "by construction" because reset only asserts under a gated
clock; that holds for the synchronous-reset CPU but not for the GPU.
\code{test_soc_gpu_isolation_via_pmu} asserts the actual (state-lost)
behaviour and a successful relaunch; tracked as a
documentation/\allowbreak{}model-fidelity bead (the physical behaviour
of a power-gated domain with no retention cells is state loss anyway).

\subsection{Results}\label{tm:flow:soc-coverage-report:results}

Basis (re-measured 2026-10-09, bead \bead{7ovx}): ONE full local
\texttt{make\ soc\_\allowbreak{}coverage} on branch
\code{test/register-walk-7ovx} (59 simulation runs, 597 passed / 0
failed / 17 skipped by the CI log-grep formula, 59 \code{.dat} files
merged), rendered with the unchanged
\src{tools/verif/coverage_report.py} and the waiver file after the 7ovx
drift fix (below). The tables are therefore a single consistent
measurement; the earlier per-bead dagger rows (unit-only
\code{dma_engine}, \code{uart_controller}, \code{spi_controller}) are
superseded by it. The PR\textquotesingle s own CI \code{soc_coverage}
run supersedes these numbers.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{Combined report: SoC + CPU / cache / GPU
suites (bead \code{1eyv},
2026-10-10)}{Combined report: SoC + CPU / cache / GPU suites (bead , 2026-10-10)}}\label{tm:flow:soc-coverage-report:combined-report-soc-cpu-cache-gpu-suites-bead-1eyv-2026-10-10}

The informational rows above reflect only what the \code{soc_*} suites
incidentally hit. The CPU, Phase 3 cache and Phase 4 GPU suites were
never part of this report (the nightly \code{rtl-coverage} job measured
only the CPU modules, and its \texttt{make\ coverage} kept just the last
simulation\textquotesingle s \code{sim/coverage.dat}; GPU had no
coverage wiring at all). This section merges them in.

\subsubsection{Method}\label{tm:flow:soc-coverage-report:method-1}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Same toolchain, same flags.} \src{sim/Makefile} now takes
  \code{COVERAGE=1} exactly like the SoC Makefile:
  \texttt{-\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}coverage-\allowbreak{}line\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}coverage-\allowbreak{}toggle\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}coverage-\allowbreak{}max-\allowbreak{}width\ 256}
  in \code{COMPILE_ARGS}, a \code{_cov} suffix on every \code{SIM_BUILD}
  (idempotent, because cocotb exports \code{SIM_BUILD} and a sub-make
  would otherwise append twice; instrumented and plain \code{Vtop} can
  never be reused for each other), and one \code{<build>__<module>.dat}
  per simulation through \code{+verilator+coverage+file+}. It replaces
  the blanket \code{--coverage} and the
  every-simulation-overwrites-\code{sim/coverage.dat} behaviour.
  \code{SIM_PYTHON3} is now overridable (CI needs it).
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{make\ -\allowbreak{}C\ sim\ coverage\_\allowbreak{}cpu\_\allowbreak{}gpu}}
  runs the 9 CPU modules + Phase 3 cache suites (\texttt{make\ test})
  and \code{gpu_all} (unit, kernels, handoff, random smoke)
  instrumented, and merges them into \code{cpu_gpu_merged.dat}. The
  existing \texttt{make\ coverage} (nightly \code{rtl-coverage},
  \code{sim/coverage_merged.dat} artifact) is kept, now also with
  per-simulation \code{.dat} files instead of losing the Phase 3 ones.
\item
  \textbf{Merge at the report level, not with
  \texttt{verilator\_\allowbreak{}coverage\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}write}.}
  \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}verif/\allowbreak{}coverage\_\allowbreak{}report.\allowbreak{}py\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}dat\ soc/\allowbreak{}merged.\allowbreak{}dat\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}dat\ cpu\_\allowbreak{}gpu\_\allowbreak{}merged.\allowbreak{}dat}
  concatenates the inputs and aggregates them like instances of one run.
  The report already identified a point as (module, file, kind, line,
  column, object, source lines) with the hierarchy dropped and "hit if
  any hit", so a point of \code{rtl/cpu/...} measured by both flows is
  one point, hit if either hit it, with nothing new to reconcile. A raw
  \texttt{verilator\_\allowbreak{}coverage\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}write}
  would instead need identical absolute paths \emph{and} hierarchy keys
  to fold points, and the two flows differ in both: the SoC \code{.dat}
  records \code{tb_soc_top.u_soc.u_cpu...}, the CPU one
  \code{Vtop.rv32i_cpu_top...}, and the files carry whatever checkout
  root the run used (a developer worktree such as
  \code{.claude/worktrees/agent-xxx/}, \code{/home/runner/work/...} on
  CI, \code{../rtl/...} from \src{sim/}).
\item
  \textbf{Path normalisation} (new): a path under \code{--root} is made
  repo-relative; any other path is anchored on its
  \texttt{rtl/\allowbreak{}<soc|periph|npu|cpu|mem|gpu>/\allowbreak{}}
  component. Before, a \code{.dat} from another checkout (including
  every CI artifact opened locally) silently dropped all RTL as "outside
  root".
\item
  \textbf{No vacuous merge} (new): an input contributing zero reportable
  RTL points is an error naming the input; modules measured by more than
  one input have their point sets compared and listed under
  \emph{Cross-input point-set consistency}, flagged \code{SUSPECT} below
  80 \% overlap (a union of two disagreeing sets would double the
  denominator and under-report), and the CLI warns on stderr. The union
  is never shrunk to the overlap and hits are never credited across
  non-matching points.
\item
  \textbf{Versions.} The nightly \code{rtl-coverage} job builds
  Verilator \textbf{5.036} from source; \code{soc_coverage} uses the
  flake-pinned nix \textbf{5.048}. Point keys are not guaranteed to
  match across them (not verifiable here: only 5.048 is installed). So
  the combined CI run does \emph{not} consume the
  \code{rtl-coverage-report} artifact: it runs \code{coverage_cpu_gpu}
  on the same nix Verilator inside \code{soc_coverage.yml}. Measured:
  with both inputs on 5.048, \textbf{every module measured by both flows
  has an identical point set} (consistency section empty), which is the
  check that the merge is exact rather than assumed. Cost: about 14 min
  and 1.5 GB for the CPU/\allowbreak{}cache/\allowbreak{}GPU half (29
  \code{TESTS=} blocks, 207 tests, 0 failed), against 85 min for the SoC
  half.
\end{enumerate}

SoC input: the 2026-10-09 \texttt{bead\ 7ovx} full \code{soc_coverage}
run (597 passed by the CI formula, 0 failed), reused rather than re-run.
\texttt{git\ diff\ 745574e\ HEAD\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}\ rtl} is
empty, so the RTL it measured is exactly this tree\textquotesingle s,
and the tests are the \code{soc_all_ci} set at \code{394db87}. Its
\code{.dat} records another worktree\textquotesingle s absolute paths,
which is itself the path-normalisation case. Triaged-tree numbers from
this combined run are identical to the SoC-only run (the CPU/GPU suites
add nothing to \texttt{rtl/\allowbreak{}soc|periph|npu}): 42 modules,
line 1667/\allowbreak{}1669, toggle 41334/\allowbreak{}59656.

\begin{lstlisting}[language=bash]
nix develop --command make -C tb/cocotb/soc soc_coverage SIM_BUILD_ROOT=/nobackup/claude_sim_build/soc_cov
nix develop --command make -C sim coverage_cpu_gpu SIM_BUILD_ROOT=/nobackup/claude_sim_build/cov_cpu_gpu
python3 tools/verif/coverage_report.py \
    --dat /nobackup/claude_sim_build/soc_cov/coverage/merged.dat \
    --dat /nobackup/claude_sim_build/cov_cpu_gpu/coverage/cpu_gpu_merged.dat \
    --waivers tools/verif/coverage_waivers.txt --out-md combined.md --out-json combined.json
# or in one go:  make -C sim coverage_combined SOC_DAT=<soc merged.dat> SIM_BUILD_ROOT=...
\end{lstlisting}

What the combined numbers say that the SoC-only ones hid: the large
"gaps" in \code{rv32i_decode}, \code{rv32i_alu}, \code{vector_alu},
\code{shared_memory} and the GPU toggle figures were just missing
suites. What they now show as genuine, unwaived gaps (bead \bead{a5ze}
tracks the triage): \code{rv32i_csr_file} (70.5 \% line, 39.0 \%
toggle), \code{rv32i_pipeline_mem} (88.6 \%), \code{rv32i_pipeline_ex1b}
(83.3 \%, unchanged by every suite), \code{gpu_top} (86.9 \%),
\code{vector_alu} (90.5 \%), \code{warp_scheduler} (40.1 \% toggle),
\code{rv32i_interrupt_ctrl} (32.1 \% toggle, no suite moves it) and
\code{gpu_command_queue} (43.9 \% toggle). Not yet triaged: some may be
unreachable by design and need waivers, none are assumed to be.

\subsubsection{CI and recommendation}\label{tm:flow:soc-coverage-report:ci-and-recommendation}

\src{.github/workflows/soc_coverage.yml} now runs
\texttt{make\ -\allowbreak{}C\ sim\ coverage\_\allowbreak{}cpu\_\allowbreak{}gpu}
on the same nix Verilator after the SoC run (\code{continue-on-error}),
renders the combined report, appends it to the job summary and uploads
it as the separate artifact \code{rtl-combined-coverage}. The existing
\code{soc-coverage} artifact, per-flow report, \code{PASS_FLOOR} and
gate are untouched, and the nightly \code{rtl-coverage} job in
\code{random_tests.yml} is unchanged. Everything stays non-gating;
\texttt{rtl/\allowbreak{}cpu|mem|gpu} stay \textbf{informational} in the
report (the recorded user decision: triage covers
\texttt{rtl/\allowbreak{}soc|periph|npu} only; this change does not
alter it).

\textbf{Recommendation (not applied): triage \src{rtl/cpu},
\src{rtl/mem} and \src{rtl/gpu} for line coverage, still not gating.}
The reason the user\textquotesingle s decision was sound, that the
numbers were an artifact of unrelated suites, no longer holds: the
combined figures (92.3 / 96.0 / 93.7 \% line) are real and only 7 of 32
modules sit below 95 \%, a list short enough to work through the way the
triaged trees were (test-gap bead or category-\code{b} waiver per
point). Toggle should stay out of any triage for these trees for now:
the CPU\textquotesingle s wide
CSR/\allowbreak{}address/\allowbreak{}data buses dominate it exactly as
in the triaged trees. Promote only once the three nightly flows run
together on the same Verilator for a few cycles, so a \code{SUSPECT}
consistency row would show up before anyone triages against it.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{rtl/cpu \textbar{} mem \textbar{} gpu line
triage, slice 1 (bead \code{a5ze},
2026-10-10)}{rtl/cpu \textbar{} mem \textbar{} gpu line triage, slice 1 (bead , 2026-10-10)}}\label{tm:flow:soc-coverage-report:rtlcpu-mem-gpu-line-triage-slice-1-bead-a5ze-2026-10-10}

Scope decision (user, 2026-10-10): triage every uncovered line point of
the modules under 95 \%, write the missing tests, then move
\texttt{rtl/\allowbreak{}cpu|mem|gpu} into the line gate. \textbf{Tests
and waivers only, no RTL edits.} \textbf{Slice 1 did not flip the gate}
(\code{gpu_top} was at 88.8 \%); slice 2, below, did.

\textbf{Basis and its limit.} Measured on HEAD \code{024abfe} with
\texttt{make\ -\allowbreak{}C\ sim\ coverage\_\allowbreak{}cpu\_\allowbreak{}gpu}
(nix Verilator 5.048) before and after the new tests: \textbf{207 passed
/ 0 failed before, 222 passed / 0 failed after} (+15 = 8 + 3 + 3 + 1;
the 16th new test is the \code{expect_fail} below, which the CI grep
does not count). The SoC half is the \code{soc-coverage} artifact of the
\prref{246} run 38029069277 (RTL \code{c68f91b}), reused rather than re-run
(75 min).
\texttt{git\ diff\ c68f91b\ HEAD\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}\ rtl} is
four lines (the \code{default:} arms \prref{248} added to
\code{rv32i_pipeline_mem.sv} and \code{rv32i_pipeline_ex1c.sv}), which
\textbf{shifts the line numbers of those two files}, so the SoC
\code{.dat} cannot be unioned point-for-point with the CPU one for them
(the report\textquotesingle s consistency section flags
\code{rv32i_pipeline_ex1c} 51/69 shared). For those two modules the
table uses the CPU/GPU \code{.dat} alone (exact at HEAD, a lower bound
because the SoC firmware may add hits); every other module is the exact
SoC + CPU/GPU union. The nightly job, which runs both halves on one
HEAD, is authoritative. Note the cost of dropping the SoC half:
\code{rv32i_cpu_top}, \code{rv32i_forwarding_unit},
\code{rv32i_hazard_unit} and \code{rv32i_dcache} fall under 95 \% on the
CPU/GPU \code{.dat} alone and are above it only because of SoC hits, so
\textbf{the line gate must be evaluated on the combined data}.

not include \code{TEST_MODULES}, only the nightly coverage job does. No

\code{soc_coverage.yml} counts \code{soc_all}; the \code{phase2_all}
floor of 100 is unaffected).

\subsubsection{Waivers added}\label{tm:flow:soc-coverage-report:waivers-added}

12 entries in \src{tools/verif/coverage_waivers.txt}
(\code{rv32i_csr_file} 3 entries / 6 points, ex1b 1, ex1c 2 / 3 points,
mem 2 / 3 points, icache 3, \code{gpu_top} 1), all category b with
file:line justification, all bound (no stale waiver). 17 line points
removed from the denominators. Line numbers are HEAD \code{024abfe}.

\subsubsection{Remaining (slice 2) and what is not verified}\label{tm:flow:soc-coverage-report:remaining-slice-2-and-what-is-not-verified}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \code{gpu_top} register / ERROR-state paths (12 points) and a
  nested-divergence kernel that overflows the 4-deep stack at
  \code{gpu_top} level; \code{icache} \code{L498}; the open
  \code{decode} \code{L508} point.
\item
  The gate flip itself (\code{coverage_report.py} \code{TRIAGED_TREES},
  \code{soc_coverage.yml}: the combined steps lose
  \code{continue-on-error} for the part the gate depends on,
  \texttt{if:\allowbreak{}\ always()} on artifact upload, the CPU/GPU
  input becomes a hard requirement) is deliberately \textbf{not} done
  here. It lands only when every module is \textgreater= 95 \% on a
  same-HEAD combined measurement.
\item
  Not verified: a same-HEAD SoC + CPU/GPU union (the SoC half is
  c68f91b); toggle coverage was not triaged (recommendation: keep the
  100 \% control-toggle gate off these trees until the line gate has run
  a few nights); the new CPU suites were not run on the 5.036 nightly
  Verilator.
\item
  Observation, not filed: \code{rv32i_csr_file} increments no counter in
  any cycle where a legal CSR instruction is in EX (\code{:332} vs
  \code{:444}), not only the written one. The header says only the
  written register is suppressed. Whether \code{mcycle} therefore
  under-counts is \textbf{unmeasured}; slice 2 should measure it before
  deciding on a bead.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{rtl/cpu \textbar{} mem \textbar{} gpu line
triage, slice 2: remaining gaps, same-HEAD measurement, gate flip (bead
\code{a5ze},
2026-10-10)}{rtl/cpu \textbar{} mem \textbar{} gpu line triage, slice 2: remaining gaps, same-HEAD measurement, gate flip (bead , 2026-10-10)}}\label{tm:flow:soc-coverage-report:rtlcpu-mem-gpu-line-triage-slice-2-remaining-gaps-same-head-measurement-gate-flip-bead-a5ze-2026-10-10}

\textbf{Basis.} HEAD \code{b8bb221} (after \prref{257}, the \code{47lf}
warp-count fix) plus this branch. The
CPU/\allowbreak{}cache/\allowbreak{}GPU half is a full local
\texttt{make\ -\allowbreak{}C\ sim\ coverage\_\allowbreak{}cpu\_\allowbreak{}gpu}
(nix Verilator 5.048). The SoC half is the \code{soc-coverage} artifact
of the green \texttt{SoC\ Coverage} run 38045855126 on \prref{257}\textquotesingle s branch (\code{c44f5ec});
\texttt{git\ diff\ c44f5ec\ origin/\allowbreak{}main\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}\ rtl}
is \textbf{empty}, so its RTL is HEAD\textquotesingle s RTL and the
point sets agree (the report\textquotesingle s consistency section is
empty). That closes the slice-1 caveat (stale SoC half for \code{ex1c} /
\code{pipeline_mem}). One honest wrinkle: the local
\code{coverage_cpu_gpu} target stopped before its merge step because one
test (\code{test_soft_reset_recovers_from_error}) was red at that
moment, for a harness reason fixed afterwards (an \code{expect_fail}
test must fail with \code{AssertionError}, it raised
\code{TimeoutError}); I merged the 32 per-simulation \code{.dat} files
by hand with the same
\texttt{verilator\_\allowbreak{}coverage\ -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}write}
the target runs, and re-ran that kernel suite on its own (6/6 pass). The
nightly job runs the unmodified target end to end.

\textbf{Result of the flipped gate on that data:
\texttt{GATE\ PASS:\allowbreak{}\ 74\ gated\ modules}} (42 triaged + 32
in \texttt{rtl/\allowbreak{}cpu|mem|gpu}), 0 below the 95 \% line floor,
no consistency warning. Per tree (line \%, with waivers): \src{rtl/cpu}
99.3 (548/\allowbreak{}552), \src{rtl/mem} 97.8 (268/\allowbreak{}274),
\src{rtl/gpu} 98.7 (297/\allowbreak{}301). \textbf{Every \code{gpu_top}
point is closed or waived (100 \%).} Lowest modules, i.e. the ones with

\code{rv32i_cache_arbiter} 96.3, \code{rv32i_cpu_top} 96.9,

\subsubsection{Gate: what is and is not covered}\label{tm:flow:soc-coverage-report:gate-what-is-and-is-not-covered}

\begin{itemize}

  Gated for line: all 32 modules of
  \texttt{rtl/\allowbreak{}cpu|mem|gpu}. No module needed a named
  exception, so no line-floor exception file exists; if one ever must (a
  module that cannot reach 95 \% without an RTL change) it gets an entry
  tied to its bead rather than a lower floor.
\item
  Not gated, deliberately: control toggle on these trees (see "Gate
  decision"). Toggle was not triaged.
\item
  \textbf{The real validation of the flip is the \texttt{SoC\ Coverage}
  job on this PR.} It touches the workflow, so the full job runs: SoC
  half, CPU/GPU half, combined report, gate. What was verified locally
  is the script (\src{tb/tests/test_coverage_report.py}, tests written
  first) and the gate result on the same-RTL data above; the workflow
  YAML itself was only parsed, not executed.
\item
  The CPU suites still run only in the nightly / this job
  (\texttt{make\ -\allowbreak{}C\ sim\ test}), not per PR; the gate is
  what makes a regression there visible.
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{The prover: waivers cite a proof, not a netlist lookup}\label{tm:flow:soc-coverage-report:the-prover-waivers-cite-a-proof-not-a-netlist-lookup}

\src{tools/verif/proven_const_bits.py} (yosys
\texttt{opt\_\allowbreak{}dff\ -\allowbreak{}sat}) cannot decide a
register-bank flop held at its reset value by
\texttt{WMASK\ =\allowbreak{}\ 0}, because the flop has no initial
value. New \src{tools/verif/stuck_bits_formal.py} closes that gap with
an unbounded model-checking proof (SymbiYosys, ABC \code{pdr}, preceded
by a short \code{bmc3} search for quick counter-examples). For a module
it takes the bits that never moved in either direction in the merged
\code{.dat}, wraps the module, assumes only that reset is asserted in
cycles 0-1 (every simulation here resets first), leaves \textbf{every
other input free}, and asserts that each bit equals its cycle-2 value
for ever after (so a combinational bit like \code{prdata[k]} must be
independent of the free inputs too). PASS = proof for all input
sequences; FAIL = a counter-example exists = a test gap (never a
waiver); timeout = \code{unknown}, reported.
\src{tools/verif/stuck_bits_waivers.py} renders the PASS bits as waiver
lines and \textbf{refuses a proven bit that lies inside a documented
writable mask} (it would be a documentation or RTL finding); none
occurred.
\texttt{-\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}tie\ PORT=\allowbreak{}VAL} holds
inputs constant (used once, below). Both tools are unit tested in
\src{tb/tests/test_stuck_bits_waivers.py}.

Waivers added (category b), about 2 770 points net (the denominator also
moved with main\textquotesingle s own RTL changes):

\endhead

\endlastfoot

\code{interrupt_controller}, \code{pwm_controller},
\code{watchdog_timer}, \code{gpio_controller}, \code{trng},

\code{pll_subsystem} was never "unanalysable": \code{pll_clkgen.sv} (the
wrapper that selects the STUB/RNM implementation with a string
parameter, RNM arm pruned) was simply missing from the
prover\textquotesingle s source list, so the yosys-slang front end saw
an unknown module. Adding it (and the DFT mux and memory-collar sources
\code{pll_subsystem} and \code{npu_top} instantiate) makes both
analysable; the previous "reject" was a missing file, not a frontend
limitation.

\code{integ_fw/gpu_walk.hex}) & The GPU\textquotesingle s two crossbar
masters (ifetch M1, data M2) walked like \#316 walked the DMA: 16
launches with \texttt{KERNEL\_\allowbreak{}PC\ |\ 1<<k} and
\code{VLD/VST} at \texttt{(1<<k)|0x2000} (k = 12..27) with data checked
per lane and every kernel word fetch seen; boot-ROM reads (data equals
the ROM), timer COMPARE write+read through M2 -\textgreater{}
\code{axi4_to_axilite} -\textgreater{} ring, boot-ROM stores answered
SLVERR with the ROM bit-identical, unmapped loads/\allowbreak{}stores
answered DECERR, and byte-offset +1..+3 loads from the PLL1 window equal
the aligned load (the only way to put a non-zero \code{paddr[1:0]}
behind the PLL clock-domain bridge; the run ends with an aligned read so
the falls are seen: the first CI run ended on +3 and left
\code{pll_subsystem} \code{paddr[1]:1->0}, fixed after it). One flush of
16 patched kernel copies instead of one per launch keeps it at about 163
k cycles. \\

counter placed just below each 2\^{}k boundary through the simulator
handle (\code{count_q}), compare fires exactly at the carry, wrap at
2\^{}32 \\

inactive \textasciitilde0x9000, wrap only after the full period) and
PRESCALE = 0x8000 \\
\code{test_spi.py::test_rx_fifo_every_slot_every_bit} & 0xFF/0x00 fills
of the 4-entry RX FIFO, drained in order \\
\code{test_i2c.py::test_i2c_fifo_every_slot_every_bit},
\code{test_i2c_max_byte_count},
\code{test_i2c_stuck_wait_counter_runs_full_width} & 8-entry TX/RX FIFOs
with 0xFF/0x00 fills; a 255-byte write with software refill; a 140
000-clk SCL stretch with the watchdog off (stuck-wait counter through
bit 15). \prref{319} (h367) adds different I2C tests (17+ byte transfers,

covers these bit patterns, so there is no overlap. \\

read back each cycle through a full IDLE power cycle equals the
